# Decisions

One entry per decision that still shapes the code, newest first. The full
records were removed in the 2026-11-03 docs cut; each entry names the commit
history to read if you need the original reasoning (`git log --follow -- <path>`).

**How to read this log (2026-11-20).** An entry's heading states its status. Entries dated before 2026-11-08 that carry no status were decided before statuses were written into headings; they are in force unless a later entry or a "Superseded" note on them says otherwise. When a later decision replaces an earlier one, the earlier one carries a note.

**Open decisions (2026-11-20)**

| Entry | Item | What is open |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |
| 2026-11-19 — M9a: what the measurement says about the ladder gates | 2 | The minimum share of the researcher's excludes an agent must take before a rung unlocks: the principle is accepted; the researcher decided on 2026-11-20 to set the number when M9b is built. |

## 2026-11-23 — H-close-side-doors: four doors around the ledger closed (decided by the researcher 2026-11-23; items 1 to 4 PROPOSED)

Record: `docs/roadmap/milestones/H-close-side-doors.md`. The work is the researcher's decision of 2026-11-23 in the coordinator session (project review, section 3 points 2 and 4, E4 to E7): remove the old batch decisions route and its allowlist entry; remove `nexus_screen_llm` and any other pre-ledger MCP screening tool that can produce a decision outside the ledger; remove `ai_agreed_exclusion`, or refuse it and stop reporting it; no default model and a required cost ceiling on the agent-run request.

**For the researcher to decide**

1. **`nexus_screen` and `nexus_screen_reconcile` are kept.** Neither can produce a decision: `nexus_screen` writes heuristic proposals to a side file (`screening_proposals.json`) that nothing reads, and `nexus_screen_reconcile` returns a majority vote and a Fleiss' kappa over legacy batch files and writes nothing. Both are pre-ledger surfaces. *Proposed:* keep them for now. *Alternative:* remove both (two more MCP tools; the proposal store and `nexus_submit_proposal` stay).
2. **`ai_agreed_exclusion` is refused and no longer reported, not removed**, because the web app's three screens and their tests read the field and workspaces may store it. The Prepare screen still offers the option, and choosing it shows the engine's refusal. *Proposed:* remove the option from Prepare (and its mentions on Home and Agreement) in a follow-up with the owner of the web app's unit tests. *Alternative:* leave the screens as they are until M9b replaces the field with the autonomy ladder's modes.
3. **An explicit `null` ceiling is still accepted for a priced model:** a run must state `cost_ceiling_usd`, and `null` means "no ceiling". The screen sends `null` when the ceiling field is blank, so it keeps working unchanged. *Proposed:* as built. *Alternative:* require a number for a priced model; the screen would then need the ceiling field filled before a run (a web app change).
4. **`base_url` stays free:** an OpenAI-compatible run sends the records and the key to any address the request names (review E6). *Proposed:* leave it, as the brief did not include it. *Alternative:* accept only `https://` addresses and loopback, in a follow-up.

**Choices made in the build** (for the reviewer)

5. The new refusals reuse the screening API's existing 422 code `INVALID_OUTPUT` with a plain message; no refusal code was added (a new code means changing the contract's §7 table and the refusal-set test).
6. `GET /settings` forces `mode` to `human_sees_every_record` on read; `settings.json` and the journal rows of a workspace that stored the old value are left untouched (no migration of stored files). `confirm_ai_agreed_exclusion` is still accepted in the body (the screen may send it) and has no effect.
7. The methods paragraph's section 6 and `figures["mode"]` are removed; the `mode` and `sample_audit_size` timelines are no longer read for it.
8. The provider and model check runs first, then the ceiling check (only for a request that can spend: a run, a dry run, a calibration); both before any state is read or written.
9. Tests that relied on the run defaults send them explicitly through one constant (`RUN` in `tests/console/test_screening_api_v1.py`); their assertions are unchanged.
10. The route's own tests are deleted with it; the two tests that used it to set up a draft write the same file shape directly, so the collector's reading of it stays tested.
11. `LLMBatchScreener` (scholar_search) loses its only caller outside its module; not removed here.

## 2026-11-23 — Findings 45, 46, 48 and 50 to 53 of the sensor-stress run: what is done with each (answered by the researcher 2026-11-23)

Record: `docs/roadmap/acceptance-run-sensor-stress-2026-11-20.md`, findings 43 to 53. Asked in the main session on 2026-11-23, each with a recommendation; all answered as recommended. Findings 43, 44 and 49's label are `H-adopted-decisions-disclosure`; finding 47 is `H-screening-read-performance`. Nothing is built by these answers; the start of the build is the researcher's to approve.

1. **Finding 45: publication is confirmed beside the button.** A "Published" line with the counts and the date next to "Publish the screening", and the page scrolls to the top status. *Not chosen:* going to Export after publishing; leaving it.
2. **Finding 46: the pilot card is shown only while few records are decided**, then one quiet line; the list of batch files folds to a count with "show all". *Not chosen:* hiding the card only; leaving it. *Not decided:* the number that counts as "few".
3. **Finding 48: "Charting" is always in the top menu.** Before publication it opens a page saying charting starts after the screening is published, with a link to Publish. *Not chosen:* hidden until published.
4. **Findings 50, 51, 52 and 53 are all to be fixed:** compact rows in Charting's Full text view; the form panel scrolls under the mouse wheel unless the Definition box has the focus; the request that answered 422 while Charting loaded is found and fixed or explained; Inception step 5 keeps one of its two sentences. None was dropped.

## 2026-11-23 — H-screening-read-performance: the build's own choices (items 6 to 23 accepted by the researcher 2026-11-23 as built; item 23's deferred run done the same day)

Record: `docs/roadmap/milestones/H-screening-read-performance.md`, "Build 2026-11-23". Items 1 to 5 are the specification's closed questions (entry of 2026-11-22). Each item below is how the build did something the specification leaves open; each is the researcher's to accept or change.

**Answered 2026-11-23.** Asked in the main session: items 6, 11 and 16 one by one, each with the recommendation "accept as built", and the remaining items together; the researcher accepted items 6 to 23 as built and approved the push, the PR and the merge at 12 of 12. *Not chosen:* item 6, the methods paragraph as a display route; item 11, an added self-check command, or dropping the shortcut; item 16, the web app deriving the Import step from the next step. **Item 23's deferred run was made by the main session the same day**, before the answer, on a copy of the sensor-stress review (record, "Measured on a copy of the review, 2026-11-23"): every measured route's answer is byte-identical between `main` `ac6d29d8` and the build `154da1a2`, apart from `computed_at` on two routes and the 443 abstracts item 13 removes; that includes `/screening/corpus` (item 11's known limit, for this review only).

6. **Where the store applies: the routes that show a state, and nothing else.** A route enters it by `read_store.display_route`: `GET /ui/publication-status`, `/review/next-step`, `/screening/prisma/preview`, `/screening/corpus`, `/screening/summary`, `/workspaces`, `/charting`, `/charting/records/{id}`, `/discovery/checks`, `/discovery/handoff`. Everything else reads the disk as before: Approve, Publish (collect), every write, the chase start, the export downloads, `/screening/methods` and `/ui/publication/outputs/{name}`. *Choice:* export and the methods paragraph are treated like Publish (they leave the app), not as display routes; the methods paragraph keeps about 0.3 s on the fixture.
7. **The key is every file under the workspace**, by name, size and modification time, apart from six top-level folders no kept result reads (`pdfs`, `extracted`, `rag`, `synthesis`, `.cache`, `charting`); a test runs every kept result and fails if it opens a file in them or outside the workspace. *Not chosen:* keying each result on the files it read (a file that appears later, such as a new decisions file, would not be seen). Cost: one `os.stat` per file per lookup (about 28 ms for the fixture's 295 files on this PC).
8. **What is kept:** the publication status, `require_current_generation`'s result, the corpus view, the screening summary and the stale-projection list; up to 32 workspaces, no time limit, nothing on disk. A result is kept only when the key read before and after computing it is the same; each caller gets its own copy.
9. **Inside one display call each file is opened once** (the per-request part of the store), and its bytes are reused only while its size and modification time are unchanged. This, not the kept results, is what brings a first call under 150 files.
10. **`require_current_generation` is asked once per display call** through `currentness.current_generation`, at its three callers (the status, `export.load_published`, the chase's publication state); the authority module is unchanged.
11. **An accepted corpus is answered from its acceptance only when that is certain:** an import manifest (exports accepted alone), round 1, the staged files' ids and sha256 equal to the manifest's and each stored file hashed again, the snapshot built under the current protocol (so under its de-duplication method), and the record counts of the manifest, the staged files and the snapshot equal. Then identified = the snapshot's records and unique = its studies. Otherwise, and when the protocol cannot be read (review finding 2), the exports are parsed and de-duplicated as before. A combined corpus (M3f) or a chase round never shows the staged counts, so they are not computed there. The de-duplicator is still constructed, so an invalid method block refuses as before. *Known limit:* the snapshot de-duplicates `verified.json` rows and the old preview de-duplicated the parsed exports; equal on the fixture (2,181 → 1,062), not proven equal for every input.
12. **`GET /screening/ui/batches/review`** answers every batch in `/ui/batches` order; each item is what `/ui/batch/{n}/review` answers, and a batch that route refuses carries `{batch_index, status, detail}`. Publish fails on the first refusal, as it did when one of its requests failed.
13. **`GET /charting` leaves out each row's `abstract`** (no screen reads it there; the one-study view reads `/charting/records/{id}`): 636 KB to 238 KB on the fixture. The other fields no screen reads (authors, venue, url, arXiv id, generation id, title-and-abstract decision and reason, round; the table-level publication and rounds) are kept: small, and other readers may use them.
14. **Same-page duplicates are removed in the request helper, not page by page:** `readJson` lets readers of the same address that start in the same synchronous run (one render's effects, as the steps bar and Home on mount) share one request; a read that starts after anything awaited, such as a write, always sends its own (review finding 1: a reload after a second quick change must not get the answer to the first). Each reader gets its own copy, an aborted reader fails as before (`CONSOLE_UNREACHABLE`), the last reader leaving stops the request, and nothing is kept after the answer. A test's own `fetch` never joins an earlier one.
15. **"The next-step bar and the page share the publication status" is done in the engine:** the bar never asked for `/ui/publication-status`; it asks for the next step, which computes the status, and the status route then reads the kept result.
16. **The screening steps bar still asks for the corpus on every screening page** (it decides whether the Import step shows); on Home it now shares the page's own request. *Not chosen:* reading the corpus state from the next step on the client (a domain decision in the web app).
17. **The loading frame** (question 5): the page's own name line (its eyebrow, already in en, fr, ar) as the level-1 heading, the sentence the page had, and two empty cards (three on Home), on Home, Import, Prepare, Agent proposals, Agreement, Publish, Export and the pilot page. Triage, Charting (its pulsing skeletons stay) and Workspaces are unchanged.
18. **Three duplicates named by the study are not in the current code**, so nothing changed for them: the pilot page asks for the summary and criteria once (the pair is on Prepare's pilot card); the one-study view does not read the table again on each move (only when it opens); the bar does not ask for the publication status.
19. **The generated fixture** (`tests/console/_sensor_stress.py`): five exports staged and accepted alone (2,181 records, 1,062 studies), 54 batches of 20, 1,062 decisions and 38 changed ones (1,100 events), one screener (`researcher:local`), projected, approved and published; no agent proposals, no pilot, no chase round, no harvest. Its file counts before the change match the study's on the real review (3,320, 3,463, 3,301).
20. **Files opened are counted as the interpreter's `open` events under the workspace**, on a first call with the store empty and the modules already imported; `os.stat` is not counted.
21. **The web app's request tests answer each page from a real engine's recorded answers** on a published three-record workspace (`apps/research-ui/tests/fixtures/page-engine.json`, written by `tests/console/_record_page_engine.py`).
22. **`stale_projections` reads each decisions file once and decodes it as UTF-8** (as `read_text` did), then compares the same bytes as before; a failure to read the context, the log or `project.json` counts every event-sourced batch stale, as the same failure in each batch's own read did.
23. **DoD 6 and 9 are recorded on the generated fixture, and their run on a copy of the sensor-stress review is deferred** (review round 2, finding 3): this session was not allowed to read the review. The fixture reproduces its file counts (item 19); its times are this PC's. *Deferred:* the same two scripts on a copy of the review, by a session allowed to make one.

## 2026-11-23 — M12c agent-proposed cells: the second reading, items 2 to 5 (answered by the researcher 2026-11-23)

Record: `docs/roadmap/milestones/M12c-agent-cells.md`, "Amendment 2026-11-23". Asked in the main session on 2026-11-23, one question per item, each with a recommendation; items 2, 3 and 5 answered as recommended, item 4 not. They close the open items of the entry "2026-11-22 — M12c agent-proposed cells: a blind second reading". Nothing is built or spent by these answers; M12c's start stays the researcher's to approve.

2. *(Answered as recommended.)* **A disagreement is defined by column type.** One of a list, and yes/no: the two readings chose different options. Number: the values differ after units and number format are normalised. Free text: never compared automatically; both are shown, and the cell is flagged only when one reading says "not reported" and the other gives a value. For every type, "not reported" against a value is a disagreement. *Known cost:* a difference between two free-text values is the researcher's to see. *Not chosen:* exact text match for every type; a third AI call that judges whether two values mean the same.
3. *(Answered as recommended.)* **One proposal when the readings agree, both when they do not.** Agreed: one proposal marked "two readings agree", with both quotes available. Disagreed: both values side by side with their quotes, and a flag. The counts and the methods text call it agreement between two readings, never correctness. *Not chosen:* always both; the first reading with a flag.
4. *(Answered "A different model"; the recommendation was the same model and prompt.)* **The second reading is made by a different model**, chosen by the researcher on the run screen. *Known cost, said when asked:* a second provider consent and key where the second model is another provider's, and a cost per reading that differs by model; the estimate, the typed ceiling and the hard ceiling name each reading's model and spend separately. *Not chosen:* the same model with the same prompt; the same model with a reworded prompt. *Not decided by this answer (the main session's reading, to confirm before M12c-2):* whether a second model of the same provider is enough, and what the run screen does when only one model is available (refuse the run, or offer a single reading labelled as such).
5. *(Answered as recommended.)* **Over MCP the app accepts one reading and labels it.** Proposals that came by one reading are marked "one reading"; a second reading is accepted when the client sends it as a separate run, and is then compared by item 2's rule. The methods text states how many cells had one reading. The app cannot verify that two client runs were independent or which model made them, beyond what the client declares. *Not chosen:* hide MCP proposals until a second run arrives; no second reading over MCP.

## 2026-11-23 — UI unit tests wait 3 s by default; the performance specification is widened (items 1 and 2 accepted by the researcher 2026-11-23)

Asked in the main session on 2026-11-23, both with the recommendation "yes"; the researcher accepted both and approved the push and the merge.

1. **`findBy…` and `waitFor` give up after 3 s, not 1 s** (`apps/research-ui/tests/setup.ts`), and a test may run 15 s, not 5 (`vitest.config.ts`). On 2026-11-22 a first-render wait past 1 s failed a different file on each full run, locally and in CI on documents-only PRs (`inception-workbench` three times, `inception-explore`, `discovery-proposals`, `proposals`). Known cost, said when asked: a render that became slower, up to 3 s, no longer fails a test; these tests check behaviour, and speed is measured by `H-screening-read-performance`. This is the alternative named in M12a Part F item 68, which gave 5 s to two first waits only; those two stay as they are. *Not chosen:* leave the default and repair each wait as it fails.
2. **`H-screening-read-performance` is amended** (its record, "Amendment 2026-11-23") from a read-only study of every page: the next-step bar first; Charting's two read routes, `/workspaces` and `/discovery/checks` in scope; pages stop repeating requests; three additions to the definition of done. Its closed questions 2 to 5 stand; question 1's "Charting measured first" is replaced. A C JSON parser showed no reliable gain and an array library does not apply; a C string-matching library for the duplicate detector is left as a later, separate decision.

## 2026-11-22 — H-charting-focus-fault: a focus asked for after a save waits for the researcher to still be there (items 1 and 2 accepted by the researcher 2026-11-23)

Record: `docs/roadmap/milestones/H-charting-focus-fault.md`. The fault itself (after Ctrl+Down the next study sometimes opened with the focus nowhere) is repaired so that the screen does what spec 02 "Keyboard" already says; that needs no decision. These two choices are not settled by the accepted specs; none is accepted until the researcher answers.

**Answered 2026-11-23.** The researcher accepted items 1 and 2 as built ("accept items 1 and 2", in this build's session) and approved the push and the PR. Where an item below still reads "PROPOSED", this answer replaces it.

1. **PROPOSED. A focus asked for after a save lands only if the researcher is still there.** Alt+N and Alt+A put the focus on the field's special button, and Enter moves it to the next field, once the save has answered. They now do so only if the same study is on screen and the focus is still in that field, or nowhere (the input was just switched off). If the researcher moved during the save (Ctrl+Down, J or Next, a click or Tab to another field), the focus stays where they went. *Alternative:* always move the focus when the save answers, as before: the researcher is pulled back to the old field, or onto the same field of the next study, after they had moved on.
2. **PROPOSED (test only). The browser test checks Esc's page, not the address a moment later.** After Esc the table opens at `row=STU-006`: it reads `row` from the address, drops it from the address (the address keeps only the view and the filter, by design), and focuses that row once the table has loaded. The test now waits for the navigation to that address instead of reading the address afterwards, which raced with the rewrite; the row is still checked by the focused STU-006 cell. *Alternative:* the table keeps `row` in the address: a product change made for a test's sake.

## 2026-11-22 — M12a Part C: export and reporting, the engine and the screens (items 1 to 4, 6 to 25 and 27 to 29 accepted by the researcher 2026-11-22 as built; items 5 and 26 changed by the researcher, built)

Record: `docs/roadmap/milestones/M12a-charting-table.md`, "Build record", Part C. Spec: `docs/ux/phase3-extraction/final/04-export-and-methods.md`, FINAL (questions 1 to 4 answered by the researcher on 2026-11-22, each as recommended). The build's own choices where record (h) and the specs left the detail open; the researcher answered them on 2026-11-22 in the main session ("Go with your recommendation and merge", the coordinator's recommendation): items 1 to 4, 6 to 25 and 27 to 29 accepted as built (the French and Arabic words, and the Arabic plural forms, stay owed: the researcher's reading); items 5 and 26 changed, as marked; push and merge approved once both changes were built. Items 1 to 17 are the engine's, 18 to 26 the screens', 27 to 29 the repairs after review round 1 (items 5 and 7 amended then). Nothing here changes a Contract v1 file.

1. **openpyxl joins the base install** (answer 8, "Add openpyxl"): `openpyxl>=3.1.5` in `dependencies`; the lock already held 3.1.5 (Docling's, the `extract` extra), so the lock changed by two lines, resolved offline.
2. **The spreadsheet has six sheets**: Charting, Full text, PRISMA counts, Charting form, About (record (h)'s five) and **Methods** (the paragraph, its sentences, the notes and what is not stated). Alternative: the five sheets, the paragraph on screen only.
3. **Every text cell of the spreadsheet is text**, never a formula: a title or a cell value starting with `=` is written as text (openpyxl would otherwise store it as a formula); numbers are numbers only in the PRISMA counts and the About counts. Control characters a workbook cannot hold are removed. The CSVs keep every value as it is (screening's convention; spec 04, question 3, answered 2026-11-22: the page says the spreadsheet is the file for Excel, item 18).
4. **`charting/prisma_fulltext.json` is written at every export** (CSV or spreadsheet), with the export time; not on every change and not by `GET /charting/methods`, which writes nothing. Screening's `prisma_report.json` is never written (DoD 16). Alternative: write it on every change of a status or a decision.
5. *(Changed by the researcher 2026-11-22: the one-box rule is kept, and the page shows the decided-from-the-abstract lines with the counts. Built: a second small table under the counts table on `/charting/report`, captioned "Not retrieved, decided from the abstract. Not a PRISMA box: these reports are also counted under Reports not retrieved above.", with Databases, Citation chasing and Total, the rows "Reports not retrieved, decided from the abstract", "of which included", "of which excluded", in en, fr and ar; absent when there is none; spec 04 says so.)* **The counts are of reports**, per round, per arm (Databases, Citation chasing) and in total: sought, not retrieved with reasons, assessed, excluded with reasons, included (scope (c)). Beside them, labelled as not PRISMA counts, what is still open: awaiting retrieval (To get or Requested), fetched and waiting for a confirmation, retrieved and not assessed, and decided without a retrieved full text. Each report is in exactly one PRISMA box, so sought = not retrieved + assessed + awaiting retrieval + retrieved and not assessed, as a PRISMA 2020 flow draws it (review of Part C, finding 1): a report Not retrieved stays there whatever its decision; "assessed" is a report with a full-text decision that is not Not retrieved (a decision taken while it was To get or Requested is counted and stated as a note). A report not retrieved and decided from its abstract is counted apart (included, excluded), said in its own sentence and shown in the spreadsheet's PRISMA sheet under "Not retrieved, decided from the abstract"; its exclusion is not a PRISMA exclusion. ~~The page's counts table does not show that line (the paragraph says it). Alternative: count such a report as assessed and say the overlap where the numbers appear.~~ (Superseded by the change above: the page shows the line.)
6. **Reasons**: not-retrieved reasons as their words; exclusion reasons keyed by code in the file, and in the paragraph as "criterion words (code): n", most frequent first; Other as "Other".
7. **The methods sentences** follow record (h) with these choices: singular and plural agree ("1 was not retrieved", "1 study"); "none is recorded as not retrieved" for zero; a sentence "n reports not retrieved were decided from the abstract: i were included and e were excluded" (item 5); "checked by the reviewer" (or "a reviewer" when more than one recorded confirmations); the open-access sentence (Part F) names the services the look-ups asked, read from their start rows in the journal, counts the confirmed copies in use and the discarded ones with their reasons, and reads "Open-access copies were looked up with …; none was used" when none is in use; "No report has been assessed at full text yet." before the first decision; "using a form that was not piloted" when no cell was charted in the pilot; each amendment as "on DATE (changes in words: reason)"; the Not reported sentence only when a cell says Not reported, and an equivalent Not applicable sentence when one does; "charted from the abstract only because no full text was retrieved" counts reports not excluded, not retrieved (any status but Retrieved) with at least one cell; a closing sentence "The counts are of reports; reports of the same study were not linked."
8. **The reviewer sentence comes from the events**: the number of screeners who recorded full-text decisions (and, for charting, cells); one is "by one reviewer"; more is "by n reviewers, each decision recorded by one of them" (no second-reviewer workflow exists yet).
9. **Notes** (shown under the paragraph and in the Methods sheet): reports still open, copies waiting for a confirmation, reports decided without a retrieved full text, cells not charted and how many are in required columns, a pilot not ended. "Not stated": with more than one round, the sentences give totals and the counts are split by round.
10. **The export's journal row** `CHARTING_EXPORTED`: parameters `format`, `view` (null for the spreadsheet), `file_name`, `sha256`; metrics `rows` (the Charting rows for the spreadsheet), `not_charted`, `cells_open`, `required_open`; outputs `charting/prisma_fulltext.json`.
11. **The screen's last export comes from `charting/last_export.json`**, written with the journal row, not from reading the journal on every `GET /charting` (the journal of a real review holds thousands of rows). The journal stays the record. Alternative: read the journal.
12. **The Full text CSV and sheet gain four columns at the end**: `file_origin` (Attached or Fetched), `file_source`, `file_version`, `file_licence` (the last three for a fetched copy; record (h) with Part F).
13. **No new refusal code**: a format other than `csv` or `xlsx`, or a CSV view other than `fulltext` or `charting`, is `CHARTING_EXPORT_FORMAT_INVALID` and writes nothing; `view` is ignored for the spreadsheet; a CSV without `view` is the Full text view (Part A's default). Part A's test that refused `format=xlsx` now refuses `format=ods`.
14. **The workbook's document dates are the export time** and its author "Nexus Scholar", so its bytes depend on the data and that time only.
15. **The About sheet**: the workspace folder's name (never its path), the form's protocol fingerprint, the newest accepted corpus snapshot, each round's generation and publication time, the form version, the export time, the app version, the rows, the open and required-open cells, "Working export: changes after the export time are not in this file", and the unit (reports).
16. **The Charting form sheet**: one line per column (name, definition, type, options, required, id), then one line per recorded amendment (date, the changes in words, the reason, whether during the pilot); "None recorded" when there is none.
17. **The methods block names the files it is computed from** ("What this statement is based on", as screening's paragraph): each round's kept-record file, the charting events files, the form, its amendments and the fetch manifest, each with its SHA-256 (`sources` in `GET /charting/methods`).
18. **The help line** (spec 04, question 3): "To read or share the table, open the spreadsheet (.xlsx) in Excel or LibreOffice. The CSV files are for programs such as R or Python; a spreadsheet program may read some values in them as formulas." (`charting.report.help`, en, fr and ar).
19. **Digits**: the report page writes Latin digits in every language (spec 04); the charting table keeps the locale's digits, so Arabic shows Arabic-Indic digits there and Latin ones on the report page.
20. **The counts table** always shows the four not-retrieved reason rows, zeros included; exclusion reasons show only when counted, sorted by code, with the protocol's words beside the code.
21. **Offline on the report page** says "Nothing is lost" with "As of {time}" (the page saves nothing, so 01's "last change saved at" does not apply).
22. **Copy keys beyond the spec**: `charting.report.counts.col.what`, `charting.report.counts.other`, `charting.report.methods.copyFailed`, `charting.report.methods.file`, `charting.report.methods.digest`, `charting.report.figures.*` and `charting.report.refused` (a typed refusal with no card of its own, its code in Details).
23. **While a file is being made**, all three download buttons are off and the busy one reads "Preparing the file".
24. **"Still open"** shows the totals only (four lines), not split by round or arm.
25. **On a wide screen** the Download column sits beside the counts but comes first in the tab order.
26. *(Changed by the researcher 2026-11-22, as spec 04 lists it. Built: Details names the protocol fingerprint, from the form (`form.json`), the value the spreadsheet's About sheet gives; "not recorded" when the form has none; en, fr and ar.)* ~~**Details shows no protocol fingerprint** (spec 04 lists it): the methods answer does not carry one; the spreadsheet's About sheet has it.~~
27. **`GET /charting/methods` is a pure read** (review of Part C, finding 2): it reads the table without seeding the form or appending the kept history (`table(ws, seed=False)`), so a first call on a table never opened writes nothing.
28. **The export lines take the singular** through the shared plural table (`lib/plural.ts`, en, fr and ar; review, finding 5): "1 row", "1 cell is not charted yet", "1 required cell is empty"; `cc()` takes an optional count.
29. **"Still open" says where a waiting copy is counted** (review, finding 3): "A copy waiting for you is also counted under its row's status above; a report assessed before its full text was retrieved is also among the assessed", and the line reads "Assessed before the full text was retrieved".

## 2026-11-22 — H-screening-read-performance: the engine reads once and keeps what it computed (items 1 to 5 answered by the researcher 2026-11-22, each as recommended; a specification, nothing built)

Record: `docs/roadmap/milestones/H-screening-read-performance.md`. Measured on the published sensor-stress review: Screening home's last API answer arrives after 9.8 s; `/review/next-step` takes 3.9 s alone and opens about 3,300 files, because the decision log and the context are reloaded once per batch, the latest decision of a record is found by scanning every event, and an accepted corpus is re-parsed and de-duplicated on every call. Disk is about a quarter of the time. Asked in the main session, one by one. No route changes its answer; no Contract v1 file changes.

1. **Scope: screening's read routes and the next-step bar**; the rest is measured first. *Not chosen:* everything in one build.
2. **Results are kept between requests**, keyed by file names, sizes and modification times; never written to disk. Lane RELEASE. *Not chosen:* per request only.
3. **Approve and Publish always recompute from disk**; display routes trust the store. *Not chosen:* a content-hash key.
4. **No combined route**; requests keep queuing.
5. **Loading state: headings and empty cards**, no animation.

The researcher also decided the order of the builds waiting behind M12a Part C: the lost-focus fault in charting (`tests-browser/charting-study.spec.ts:209`, which failed twice in CI on a documents-only PR), then this, then `H-adopted-decisions-disclosure`; and that the first may start in a second session while Part C finishes, the sessions taking turns on heavy runs.

## 2026-11-22 — M12a Part F: open-access fetching, the engine and the screens (items 1 to 68 accepted by the researcher 2026-11-22 as built)

**Answered 2026-11-22.** The researcher accepted items 1 to 68 as built (asked in the main session with the five items it singled out: 15, 22, 51, 60 and 68; the answer was to go with its recommendation, accept all) and approved the push and the merge at 12 of 12. On item 22 the change is taken (the provider keeps an old-style id's archive); a read-only check the same day found no old-style arXiv id among the 2,176 verified records of the researcher's review. Item 51 stays owed: the French and Arabic words of the fetch screens have not been read by the researcher. Where an item below still reads "PROPOSED", this answer replaces it.

Record: `docs/roadmap/milestones/M12a-charting-table.md`, "Build record", Part F. Spec: `docs/ux/phase3-extraction/final/03-fetch-states.md` (FINAL; questions 1 to 10 answered by the researcher on 2026-11-22, each as recommended, the fifth refusal code `FETCH_FILE_WAITING` approved with that answer). Builds section (d2) and DoD F1 to F8 on Part K (PR 283). Items 1 to 24 are the engine's, 25 to 58 the screens', 59 to 65 the engine's repairs after review round 1 (items 6, 20 and 22 amended then), 66 and 67 after round 2 (item 60 amended then), 68 from the PR gate. The build's own choices; none is accepted until the researcher answers.

1. Confirm and discard are event kinds on `POST /charting/events` (`fetched_confirm` `{manifest_row}`, `fetched_discard` `{manifest_row, reason, reason_text}`); their undo is `value: null` with `supersedes`; a non-null value must name the waiting row.
2. A confirm makes the engine write the attachment (`origin: fetched`) and the Retrieved status in the same request (engine-made ids, marked `added_by_engine` in the results); undoing the confirm takes them back only while they still hold.
3. One code, `FETCH_FILE_WAITING` (409, spec 03 question 6), for a status, a decision or an attachment on a row with a file waiting; the screen turns it into "Confirm this PDF as the full text? Enter". Confirm and decide in one request is accepted.
4. Confirm and discard are journaled as `CHARTING_EVENTS_RECEIVED`, not `CHARTING_ATTACHMENT_ADDED`.
5. Counts as spec 03 question 1 recommends: a waiting row leaves To get / Requested / Not retrieved and counts in `to_confirm`; `counts.prisma` (sought, not retrieved, retrieved, assessed) reads the status alone and does not move until a confirm.
6. Fetched files keep the kit's names, `pdfs/fetched/DOC-<sha256[:32]>.pdf` ((d2) and spec 03 said `<sha256>.pdf`). The manifest names each file relative to the workspace (`pdfs/fetched/DOC-….pdf`), never by an absolute path: the manifest is tracked in git and must not carry the researcher's home path, and a moved or copied workspace still serves and confirms its waiting files (review of Part F, finding 1; the kit takes the root as `manifest_file_root`, item 20).
7. Selection: nothing selected = every kept To get record minus those with a file, a waiting file or a discarded file; a selection may hold To get, Requested and Not retrieved records (others left out and counted; a discarded record is asked again when selected); a record not kept is `RECORD_NOT_KEPT`; nothing to ask is `BODY_INVALID` with the plan.
8. Records left out are not "reached" and get no manifest row.
9. The same bytes as a discarded file: the row is written, the file not offered (`same_as_discarded`).
10. A record with both a DOI and an arXiv id is asked by DOI only.
11. An arXiv copy is `submittedVersion` ("preprint (arXiv vN)"), repository, source arXiv, ranked with the services' copies; the kit's three-copy cap applies.
12. arXiv look-ups: 50 ids per request without their version (the latest is answered), `max_results` set, all before the downloads; a failed request marks its ids as failed look-ups.
13. Pacing: arXiv's hosts share the API's one-per-3-s budget; other hosts a 1 s pause between two requests to the same host; one record at a time; the lookup client 5 per second, no disk cache.
14. "Try later": a PDF 429, or a look-up 409, 429 or 503.
15. One run per workspace by `charting/fetch_runs/active.lock` (process and engine named; a stale lock taken over silently); "interrupted" read from a run file left in the state running when no run of that id is live in this console, nothing written. This matches the answer to spec 03 question 8 (inferred, nothing written at start-up); the spec's sentence says "from a start with no finish in the journal", and the engine reads the run file instead, which holds the counts the card shows ("stopped after 88 of 214"). The journal's start row without a finish stays as the question's answer said.
16. The contact e-mail is the kit's (`SCHOLAR_MAILTO`, then `MAILTO`); the plan is refused without it too.
17. The start needs `confirm: true`; `ids` and `scope` together are `BODY_INVALID`.
18. `GET …/fetched` with nothing waiting is `FETCH_NOT_PENDING` (409).
19. The e-mail is written in the start journal row and the run file (never the key).
20. Kit additions with defaults that leave the commands unchanged: `max_bytes`, `max_redirects` (aiohttp gets N+1), `pacer`, `process_copies`, `record_outcome`, `process_doi(extra_copies, prior_lookups, manifest_extra)`; `OpenCopy.version_label` and a `version_label` manifest field (schema name kept v1); `manifest_file_root` (the folder a row's `file` is relative to; without it the command's rule holds: the manifest's folder, else absolute).
21. The PDF session ignores environment proxies; the Unpaywall/OpenAlex lookup client (httpx) still follows `HTTP(S)_PROXY` as the search kit does.
22. **The arXiv provider's parsing of old-style ids (DoD F7), a change to harvests, PROPOSED on its own.** *Built:* `scholar_search/providers/arxiv.py` keeps an old-style id's archive (`hep-th/9901001`; it used to keep only `9901001`). *What changes for corpora already stored:* they hold the bare numbers; a new harvest's `hep-th/9901001` no longer matches a stored `9901001` on the arXiv-id tier of deduplication (DOI and title still can match it); and within stored corpora two papers from different archives with the same number may already have been merged under one key. Nothing is migrated. *What else changes for old-style ids* (review of Part F, finding 4): the provider's `provider_id` and `external_ids.arxiv_id` are inputs of the record identity (`scholar_search/identity.py`, `_record_identity`), so the same old-style paper gets a different REC- and STU- id in a harvest made after the change than in one made before; the verifier's OpenAlex look-up by arXiv id, the citation-chasing keys and the retraction look-up receive the id with its archive (for the verifier, a repair: it can now find the paper). New-style ids keep their REC- and STU- ids. The contract baseline does not change. *Does the fetch need the provider change?* Only for records harvested after it: the fetch reads the arXiv id stored on the record, and a stored bare number cannot be turned back into its archive by any look-up, so the fetch skips it ("its arXiv id has no archive", counted) whichever way this is decided. *Alternative:* keep the provider's harvest parsing as it is and handle old-style ids only on the fetch path: no change to matching for stored or new corpora, but new harvests keep losing the archive (so old-style arXiv-only papers can never be fetched by arXiv id, and two archives' papers keep colliding on one key). *Costs:* the change as built costs a matching difference between corpora harvested before and after it; the alternative costs the old defect, kept. New-style ids (2007 on) are unaffected either way.
23. **The run names what failed** (found while building the screens): the run record carries `check_failures` (each failed file check by name, once per record) and `lookup_failures` (each failed look-up that is not "try later", by service and HTTP status or error kind), so the summary can say them in the spec's words; `errors` keeps its counts.
24. **A record no longer kept keeps its fetch state on the row** (found while building the screens: the rows no longer kept were built without it); it is not counted in "to confirm" (only kept rows are counted), and a confirm on it is refused `RECORD_NOT_KEPT`.

Screens (DoD F8; spec 03 FINAL; the choices the spec did not settle):

25. **The Show option** "Fetched, to confirm" comes after "Not retrieved", in the Full text view only; the To get, Requested and Not retrieved filters leave out rows with a file waiting, so each list matches its count.
26. **The earlier status badge stays visible** under the "Fetched, to confirm" badge (it is the status that returns on discard).
27. **The badge** is on the page's plain background (`leaf`) with a dashed `ink-muted` border, words and icon; the "Recommended" mark sits on `leaf-raised`.
28. **"{n} of {total} shown"** appears under the "to confirm" filter only.
29. **The toolbar button** is disabled, with its words visible, only when nothing is selected and no kept To get row is free of a file; with a selection it is always on; `L` in the disabled state puts the words on the status line.
30. **The plan's numbers**: the title counts the records asked; "Nothing to look up" when none has a DOI or an arXiv id; the "already" line adds files and files waiting; the Unpaywall and arXiv lines appear only above zero; a line for discarded records left out (`.plan.discarded`); "of 1 record" has its own words.
31. **The start** sends `{scope: "to_get", confirm: true}` with nothing selected and `{ids, confirm: true}` (kept rows only) with a selection.
32. **`E` on a waiting row**: Enter opens the reason chips; the confirm and the exclusion go in one request after the reason; Esc at the chips sends nothing. **Undo of a confirm with its decision** is one `Z`: the decision is taken back first, then the confirm (the engine refuses a decision on a waiting row).
33. **Bulk status keys** with waiting rows: the confirmation line is shown whenever rows are skipped, even with one row left; when every row is waiting, only the skip sentence shows.
34. **Where refusals show**: the page's own checks (status keys on a waiting row, no file waiting, one at a time, reason needed) on the status line, as Part A's local checks; the engine's refusals in the `role="alert"` banner.
35. **Buttons**: "Requests" is a button with `aria-expanded`; "Cancel the look-up", "Start the look-up" and the confirm question's Confirm are bordered secondary buttons.
36. **Announcements**: one each time 25 more records are reached; the end sentence is used for a cancelled run too.
37. **The summary's title** says "finished" or "cancelled" only when this page watched the run; otherwise "Last look-up, {when}"; Dismiss lasts for the page load (no browser storage, N12).
38. **"Select the n to try later"** selects the rows whose last look-up in that run says try later and that have no file waiting (n counts those rows), and switches to the Full text view and Show all when needed; it starts nothing.
39. **A run that crashed** (engine state `failed`) shows the interrupted card with its own sentence (`.run.failed`) and Dismiss.
40. **The table is read again** while a run moves (reached, downloaded or state), at most once per two-second poll; the one-study view re-reads its record the same way.
41. **The run card's skeleton** shows on desktop only, until the first answer.
42. **The one-study view**: the summary only for a run watched there, without Show and Select; no toolbar button (`L` only); Attach and the link are hidden while a file waits.
43. **"What was tried"** shows the newest manifest row of the record.
44. **Service answers in the panel**: the copy's own service "open copy listed" (arXiv "vN found"); another service that answered "answered" (new words) unless the outcome is "no open copy listed"; not found "no open copy listed"; a failure "failed (HTTP n)" or "failed (the connection failed)".
45. **Addresses in the panel**: at most three, in the order asked; version and licence only on the copy's own address (new key `addressShort`); redirects add "redirected n times".
46. **The PubMed Central line** shows for an `ncbi.nlm.nih.gov` or `pmc.` host that did not give the stored file; `europepmc.org` does not count.
47. **Check names in words**: `pdf_header` not a PDF; `pdf_trailer`, `structure`, `readable` the PDF structure is not valid; `size_floor` too small; `size_limit` over 100 MB; "every file check passed" when none failed, then the identity result.
48. **Row outcome words**: a PDF not fetched reads "try later" when the service asked to wait, else the failed check when the message names one, else "the download failed"; FAILED and MANIFEST_NOT_WRITTEN read "the download failed"; the same bytes as a discarded file have their own sentence.
49. **Error classes in the summary**: other 4xx "refused", 5xx "the host had an error"; certificate, time-out, network error, over the limit, too many redirects and manifest not written have their own words; a 409 look-up is never shown as "budget spent" (the engine counts it as try later).
50. **Copy keys beyond the spec's list** (en, fr, ar): the names with the title (`.openFor`, `.confirmFor`, `.discardFor`), `.plan.titleOne`, `.plan.discarded`, `.run.failed`, `.run.showProgress`, `.tried.loading`, `.tried.serviceAnswer.answered`, `.tried.notAskedKey`, `.tried.addressShort`, `.answer.*`, `.error.redirects`, `.error.lookupAny`, `.check.allPassed`, `.outcome.sameAsDiscarded`, `.discard.doneReason.*`.
51. **The French and Arabic words** of every `charting.fetch.*` key were written in the build and need the researcher's reading.
52. **The proxy** passes the waiting PDF through as bytes (it was not on the list of file answers, so it would have been read as text) and adds `nosniff` to every file answer.
53. **The phone**: the waiting file's lines and its Open link sit beside the card's own link, not inside it; in the one-study view Confirm and Discard are hidden and `L`, `C`, `D` are ignored.
54. **Two Part A tests changed** because spec 03 changes the behaviour they lock: the Show options now include "Fetched, to confirm" (`charting.states`), and `L`, `C`, `D` are now keys (`charting.keymap`, which closes the plan with Esc and still checks that nothing is sent).
55. **A record failing two checks that share a line** (for example `pdf_trailer` and `structure`, both "the PDF structure is not valid") is counted once per check, so that line can be larger than the number of records.
56. **A failed look-up with no HTTP status** is said with the time-out, certificate or connection words, without the service's name (the spec's error lines have no service slot).
57. **"A lookup failed"** shows only when the run names no look-up failure; the named entries count look-ups, not records, so their sum can differ from the records counted in `errors`.
58. **One more copy key**, `charting.fetch.summary.checkLine` ("{n} × {check}"), carries the check lines.

After review round 1 (engine):

59. **A protocol replace waits for a running look-up** (review of Part F, finding 2; spec 03 "Stale" said the engine cancels it first): genesis with `replace` is refused `WORKSPACE_JOB_ACTIVE` ("look-up of open-access copies FR-…") while this console runs one in the workspace, as for a search job; the researcher cancels the look-up or waits for its end. A cancel by the replace itself was not built.
60. **A row the researcher changes during a run** (review, finding 3): a record given a file of its own or a status a look-up does not ask about (Retrieved) after the start is not asked when the run reaches it; it leaves the records asked (`total` goes down by one, `left_out.changed_during_run` counts it) and gets no manifest row (item 8). A file that arrives for such a row while it was being fetched stays on disk and in the manifest and does not wait on the row while the row stays the researcher's (a file of their own, or Retrieved), so the counts still add up and the status keys stay free. If the row later becomes one a look-up asks about again with no file (the Retrieved undone, or set to To get, Requested or Not retrieved), that file waits on it again as "Fetched, to confirm" and the status keys ask for a confirm or discard first (review round 2, finding 1: the rule reads the row as it is now, not when the file came; hiding the file for good was the alternative, not built). The summary and the finish journal row count these rows (item 67).
61. **The Full text export says "Fetched, to confirm"** for a row whose fetched file waits (spec 03, export words; review, finding 5), in both CSVs' status column; never "To get" or "Retrieved".
62. **The start's own answer is never read as interrupted** (review, finding 6): a short run can end between the start and its answer; the 202 says `running` and the next read says how it ended.
63. **The waiting file of a record no longer kept can still be opened** (review, finding 7), as what was tried can; confirm and discard on it stay `RECORD_NOT_KEPT`.
64. **"Try later" on a row** only for a record left without a file (review, finding 8): a 429 from one service while the other gave the file is nothing to try later, so the rows "Select the n to try later" picks agree with the run's count.
65. **No finish row without a start row** (review, finding 10): when the start cannot be written to the journal, the run is released without a `CHARTING_FETCH_FINISHED` row.
66. **A half-written events file never fails a run** (review round 2, finding 2): the check of item 60 reads the events once per record; if that read fails, the record is asked as planned (a file that still arrives on a changed row is hidden by the row rule of item 60).
67. **Rows changed during a run are said** (review round 2, finding 3): the finish journal row has `changed_during_run` beside `asked`, and the summary a line "{n} changed by you during the look-up and not asked" (new key `charting.fetch.summary.changed` in en, fr and ar), shown only above zero.
68. **Two inception test files wait 5 s for their first render** (found by the PR gate, not by a review): with Part F's fourteen test files the full `vitest run` (108 files) took `inception-explore.test.tsx` and `inception-workbench.test.tsx` about 30 s each, and their first `findBy` went past the default 1 s in two of two runs; each file passes alone (three runs) and with all the charting files (two runs), and `origin/main` `46ebe639` (94 files) passed its one run. Only those two waits get `timeout: 5000`; no product code changes. The alternative, a longer default for every `findBy` in `tests/setup.ts`, would hide slow renders everywhere.

**Lane RELEASE** (new routes, a new persisted record and journal rows, refusal codes, outgoing requests, a change to the arXiv provider's parsing).

## 2026-11-22 — M11b follow-up: a placeholder workspace does not sign the not-reported rule (items 1 to 7 accepted by the researcher 2026-11-22 as built; item 8 changed by the researcher to the alternative, a line on Prepare, built; M11b-criteria-pilot, "Follow-up: placeholder workspaces")

Record: `docs/roadmap/milestones/M11b-criteria-pilot.md`, "Build record", "Follow-up: placeholder workspaces". Part D (entry "2026-11-21 — M11b part D", answers 8 and 12) gave the rule no default at step 5, Review, genesis and the terminal wizard; the researcher left the placeholder scaffold out of part D and approved its fix as a follow-up PR. The placeholder scaffold is one function, `scaffold_placeholder_workspace`, behind `nexus-scholar init --scaffold-only` and the Workspaces "Create" (`POST /api/v1/workspaces`); it asks no question and wrote `method.not_reported: "unclear"` into the signed protocol. Nothing here changes a Contract v1 file, the protocol model or schema, or an existing protocol; `generate_contract_baseline.py --check` is current. The researcher answered on 2026-11-22 in the main session ("go with recommendations": accept 1 to 7 as built; on item 8 take the alternative).

1. *(Accepted by the researcher 2026-11-22 as built; relayed by the main session.)* **A placeholder without a given rule states no method block** (neither `intent.json` nor `protocol.json` has `method`), so no rule is signed. Every reader then takes the harness defaults with source `defaults` (the rule `unclear`; the guideline from the playbook), as for a protocol written before 2026-11-08. The engine reports the source (`not_reported_source: "defaults"` in the screening settings); Prepare now says so under the rule (item 8, as changed), and the published methods paragraph says the protocol does not set it. The rule is chosen when the placeholder is replaced (New review in the app), where part D already blocks, or on Prepare by a method amendment. *Cost:* changes the 2026-11-08 sentence "New workspaces are written with the block, filled with today's values" for placeholders made without `--not-reported` (part D changed it already for step 5); such a placeholder's protocol no longer states the dedup values, the rule table, the visibility or the guideline (all read as defaults or from the playbook; the methods paragraph says "These are the harness defaults …; the protocol does not set them"); its fingerprint differs from yesterday's placeholder (the golden test now pins both values). Its visibility is then not set by the protocol: it becomes a workspace setting changed on Prepare (`PUT /settings`, journalled `SCREENING_SETTINGS_CHANGED`), and a method amendment of the visibility is refused, as for a protocol written before 2026-11-08. *Alternative not taken:* treat the placeholder as a genesis and ask or refuse there as part D does: the CLI would ask the rule, and `POST /workspaces` would refuse without it, so the Workspaces "Create" panel would need a rule question (en, fr, ar) before any criteria exist, for a protocol the screen itself calls a placeholder to replace with New review.
2. *(Accepted by the researcher 2026-11-22 as built; relayed by the main session.)* **`--not-reported` with `init --scaffold-only` signs the rule as part D's genesis does**: the intent carries `method: {blinding: reveal_after_decision, not_reported}` and the protocol the block with the playbook's guideline. Before, the option was silently ignored with `--scaffold-only`. With `unclear` the protocol is byte for byte the placeholder of before this change (same fingerprint). A value outside the three is refused (exit 2) before anything is written; the function itself refuses it `METHOD_RULE_INVALID`, a code no route can reach today (the CLI checks first, the API never passes a rule).
3. *(Accepted by the researcher 2026-11-22 as built; relayed by the main session.)* **A scripted `init --scaffold-only` without `--not-reported` is not refused**, since nothing is signed. *Alternative not taken:* refuse it as answer 12 refuses a scripted wizard run; the user guide sends automation and CI to `--scaffold-only`, which would then need the option everywhere.
4. *(Accepted by the researcher 2026-11-22 as built; relayed by the main session.)* **`POST /api/v1/workspaces` is unchanged** (title only; a workspace made there never signs a rule). *Alternative not taken:* an optional `not_reported` body field, which no screen would send.
5. *(Accepted by the researcher 2026-11-22 as built; relayed by the main session.)* **A search amendment recompiles a protocol that states no method block without one** (`compile_amendment` passes `state_method` from the current protocol), so "Change the search" on a new placeholder is not refused `outside_search` on `method`. *Cost:* the same holds for a protocol written before 2026-11-08 without the block, whose search amendments were refused `outside_search` until now; they become possible (only the search fields change; the method still reads the defaults). *Alternative not taken:* decide from `project.json`'s `protocol_origin == "placeholder"`, which keeps the old refusal for those older protocols at the price of a second reading of the workspace.
6. *(Accepted by the researcher 2026-11-22 as built; relayed by the main session.)* **Screening on a placeholder is not blocked.** Part D blocks no screening, and the brief asks for no second mechanism; the rule can be changed on Prepare by a method amendment (H-screening-drafts item 2), the next-step bar already sends a placeholder to New review (`PROTOCOL_PLACEHOLDER`), and the methods paragraph says the protocol does not set it. Prepare says under the rule that it is the harness default and nobody chose it (item 8, as changed). *Alternative not taken:* refuse a harvest or screening on a placeholder protocol: a new block, a follow-up if the researcher wants it.
7. *(Accepted by the researcher 2026-11-22 as built; relayed by the main session.)* **A New review started from a new placeholder's protocol leaves the rule unchosen** (the importer finds no block), so part D's `METHOD_RULE_UNCHOSEN` applies; a placeholder written before this change still carries its `unclear` and is shown as imported, to confirm (part D items 21 to 24). Legacy workspaces are not migrated in place.
8. *(Changed by the researcher 2026-11-22: the alternative, "add the one-line label on Prepare saying the not-reported rule shown is the default and was not chosen", only where the source is `defaults`, no new control, no engine or API change. Built.)* **Prepare says when the rule shown is the harness default.** Under the not-reported rule's note, while the engine's settings answer `not_reported_source: "defaults"`, one line reads "The rule shown is the harness default: the protocol does not set it and nobody has chosen it." (fr: "La règle affichée est la règle par défaut du logiciel : le protocole ne la fixe pas et personne ne l'a choisie."; ar: "القاعدة المعروضة هي القاعدة الافتراضية للأداة: لا يحددها البروتوكول ولم يخترها أحد."). The group's `aria-describedby` names it with the note. It is gone when the protocol sets the rule (`protocol`) or once an amendment changes it (`amendment`), read from the settings the engine answers after the amendment. It also shows for a protocol written before 2026-11-08 without the block, whose rule is the default too. The screen reads the field the settings route already answered (`screening_v1.py` `load_settings`); no engine or API change. *As proposed:* no label (Prepare showed Unclear selected like a chosen rule; review of this follow-up, finding 1).

## 2026-11-22 — M12c agent-proposed cells: a blind second reading (item 1 answered by the researcher 2026-11-22: "Yes, add it to the spec"; items 2 to 5 answered 2026-11-23, see the entry of that date)

Record: `docs/roadmap/milestones/M12c-agent-cells.md`, "Amendment 2026-11-22". Proposed by the main session after answer 7 of the entry "2026-11-21 — M12c agent-proposed cells" ("No measurement") left M12c with no check on the agent. Asked in the main session on 2026-11-22 with three choices (yes; one reading; decide when M12c starts). Nothing is built or spent by this answer; M12c's start stays the researcher's to approve.

1. *(Answered "Yes, add it to the spec", as recommended.)* **Each paper is read twice by the agent, the two readings independent of each other** (neither is shown the other's output), and the researcher still decides every cell. Where the two readings disagree, the cell is flagged for a closer look. Known cost, said when asked: about twice the AI spend per paper. *Not chosen:* one reading, as specified on 2026-11-21; leaving the question until M12c starts.

Open, to be written into the specification and asked before M12c-2 is built (not decided here):

2. What counts as a disagreement for each column type (text, number, yes/no, one of a list), and for "not reported" against a value.
3. What the researcher sees: both values with their quotes, or one proposal and a flag; and what the counts and the methods text say about agreement (agreement between two readings is not accuracy).
4. Whether the two readings use the same model and prompt, and how the estimate, the typed ceiling and the hard ceiling name the doubled spend.
5. The MCP path (answer 3, "Both from the start"): the app cannot make a client read twice or keep the readings apart; whether a second reading there is required, offered or absent.

## 2026-11-22 — M12a Part K: the PDF kit's downloading made production (items 1 to 20 accepted by the researcher 2026-11-22 as built)

Record: `docs/roadmap/milestones/M12a-charting-table.md`, "Build record", Part K. Builds section (d3) items 3, 4 and 6 to 13 on top of K0 (PR 274) and the resolver (PR 278, whose items 13 and 14 behave as merged). The build's own choices where the record left the detail open. The researcher accepted all twenty as built on 2026-11-22 (asked in the main session: items 2, 4, 11 and 20 one by one, item 17 with its own question, the other fifteen in four groups) and approved the push. On item 17 they chose "Plan it as a follow-up": PMC's own open-access route gets a specification after Part F (plan §6); nothing of it is built or decided here. The two prepared live checks are approved for after the merge, the commands shown to the researcher first. Nothing here changes a Contract v1 file.

1. **OpenAlex without its key is "not asked"**: neither an answer nor a failure; the outcome comes from Unpaywall alone (for example exit 5) and the message says "OpenAlex was not asked (SCHOLAR_OPENALEX_KEY is not set)"; the command prints a note rather than refusing. With the key both services are asked (resolver item 4).
2. **The key is sent as OpenAlex's `api_key` parameter**; `mailto` is no longer sent to OpenAlex as a parameter (the contact stays in the User-Agent). The key is never logged, printed or written to a result or the manifest, and the kit's lookups keep **no on-disk cache** (review of Part K, finding 1: the cache stored each lookup address, key and e-mail included, in plain text under the folder the command ran from). The search kit's own cache has the same exposure (a follow-up outside M12a).
3. **A 409 from OpenAlex** stays a failed lookup (exit 3, "run again later") with its own words: "failed (HTTP 409: the daily budget is spent)".
4. **Retries of a PDF address**: a connection error, a timeout or a 5xx is retried (2 to 10 s back-off, 3 attempts); a 429 is retried after its `Retry-After` when that is at most 60 s (the search kit's cap), else ends at once "HTTP 429: the host asks to try later"; 400, 401, 403, 404, 410 and 451 are asked once (PDF-06), and so are an invalid address and a certificate error (review, finding 6). Failures of a request are put in words, never the error's own text; a failure to store a file keeps its text (a local path).
5. **Closed evidence per service** (PDF-12): a service that says the work is closed (`is_oa: false`, `oa_status: closed`, at the top or in OpenAlex's `open_access`) has none of its addresses listed; the other service's open copy still counts.
6. **Access statuses**: a file through the parked institutional gateway reads `INSTITUTIONAL_ACCESS` (`was_oa` false, no open-copy label); `ingest` reads `USER_PROVIDED` (E1's name, `was_oa` false); with no file the status comes from the services' answers.
7. **The size floor stays at 10 KB** and is named as such in the message (PDF-17); the checks are reported by name in the order header, trailer, size floor, structure.
8. **The copy fallback where hard links fail** (PDF-03): on EPERM, EXDEV, EMLINK, EOPNOTSUPP/ENOTSUP, ENOSYS and the Windows codes 1, 17, 50 and 1142, an exclusive-create copy, flushed and checked against the file's hash; a partial copy is removed; any other link error still fails. E1's and E2's own links are untouched.
9. **The identity check** (PDF-22): the first two pages; the DOI as written or with line breaks removed; the title when at least 80 % of its distinct words of three letters or more appear; the caller's title first, then the looked-up one; unreadable text is "not found: check it"; without PyMuPDF "not checked". It never blocks.
10. **The manifest** (PDF-07, the kit's half): `download-manifest.jsonl` inside `--output` by default (`--manifest PATH` otherwise), rows `scholar-pdf-download-manifest-v1`; a DOI without a file has a row too; the study id from the record's `study_id`, `record_id`, `workspace_id` or `id`; a row that cannot be written ends `MANIFEST_NOT_WRITTEN`, exit 1, and the file is not kept; `ingest` writes rows too (`--manifest`, `--study-id`, outcome `NOT_STORED` on failure). The legacy `literature/pdf_manifest.json` is never written.
11. **Addresses in the manifest are kept as given**, query strings included (unlike E1's redaction, PDF-20); only a `user:password@` part is removed.
12. **Exit codes** (PDF-15): a missing `--input` file exits 2 (usage), an input that is not a JSON list exits 1, `ingest` exits 1 when it stores nothing; 3, 4 and 5 keep the resolver's meanings.
13. **Exports** (PDF-18): a BibTeX year the services do not give is left out (the key falls back to `Unknown`/`nd`); one entry per DOI across runs; the JSON export writes `null` for a missing file path; both written atomically. `--smart-names`, `_safe_filename` and `models.py` are gone (PDF-05); the parked console pipeline template no longer passes `--smart-names`.
14. **Every PDF request is visible** (asked for by the main session after the live check of PR 278): each request (address, host with its port, status or error, retry or not, redirects) is on the result and in the manifest row (`request_count`, `request_hosts`), and `download` prints per DOI how many PDF requests went to which hosts and what each answered. A request is a retry when the one before it went to the same address.
15. **The publisher patterns are deleted** (G3); `rewrite_via_proxy`, `is_proxied_url` and `PROXY_STYLES` stay as parked proxy code; `ENABLE_PUBLISHER_DIRECT_PATTERNS=true` is still refused, now saying the patterns were removed.
16. **The new modules' names** (`manifest`, `identity`, `pdf_checks`) are importable from their modules, not added to the package's exported list (E1 has `MANIFEST_*` names of its own).
17. **PubMed Central refuses its PDF to this client** (the live check of PR 278: PMC's published version was ranked first and gave no PDF; the repository's submitted version was taken). Nothing poses as a browser (items 24, 27, K0 stand); the row and the printed line say what PMC answered. *PROPOSED for a later decision, not built:* PMC's own route for its Open Access Subset (the PMC OA Web Service, `…/pmc/utils/oa/oa.fcgi?id=PMC…`, which returns package and sometimes PDF links; the DOI-to-PMCID ID Converter), recalled from memory and to be checked before any build; it covers the OA Subset only.
18. **A DOI written as a link or with `doi:` is looked up as the bare DOI** (found by the Part K build: `download --input` passed `https://doi.org/…` unchanged); its case is kept; the input file is de-duplicated on the bare DOI, so one DOI written two ways costs one lookup and one row (review, finding 8).
19. **Not in Part K, left to Part F**: a 100 MB stop on a fetched body and a five-redirect cap (aiohttp's default of ten redirects applies to the command); `download_batch` takes no study ids.
20. **The downloader refuses a forward (transport) proxy** (`PROXY_REFUSED`; review of Part K, finding 2): a file fetched through one would have been labelled open access. Accepted item 25 holds (no proxy in production); the parked institutional gateway, used only when a caller passes it, reads `INSTITUTIONAL_ACCESS`; E1's own transport (parked) is separate.

**Lane RELEASE** (a kit's public commands and network behaviour change).

## 2026-11-22 — M12a Part B follow-up: merging columns from the form panel; attach and open tested in the browser (items 1 to 7 accepted by the researcher 2026-11-22 as built)

Record: `docs/roadmap/milestones/M12a-charting-table.md`, "Build record", "Part B follow-up". The two things Part B did not build, which the researcher asked for as a follow-up after Part B merged (2026-11-22). The engine already records a merge (Part B items 4 and 5); nothing in it changes. This amends the last sentence of accepted Part B item 25 ("Merging columns in the panel is not built"). The build's own choices. The researcher accepted all seven as built on 2026-11-22 (asked in the main session, together, on its recommendation) and approved the push.

1. **The control**: each column card gets a quiet "Merge with…" after "Split into two" (disabled while the table is read-only or the form has fewer than two columns). It opens a small chooser under the card, not a dialog: a labelled select "Merge {name} with" listing the other columns in form order (the first one picked), then "Merge the two columns" and "Cancel"; Esc closes only the chooser; the focus goes to the select, back to the button on Cancel or Esc, and to the new column's name after a merge, as with a split.
2. **The new column**: Text, no options, named "{a} and {b}" in form order (fr "{a} et {b}", ar "{a} و{b}"), at most 200 characters; its definition the two joined with "; " (at most 2,000); required if either was; no protocol id; it takes the first column's place, replaces both, and its `derived_from` names both.
3. **Saving as a split does**: the same queued form save, at once before any value exists, through the amendment prompt with its sentence afterwards; the engine records "merged" and marks the new column's cells "Chart again" with each old column's earlier value as history, and both views show every one of them, one line per old column (review of the follow-up, finding 1: they showed only the last).
4. **Three or more columns merge step by step**: merging a column made by an unsaved merge uses its own sources, so A with B, then that with C before recording, gives one "merged" row with three sources; merging a merge already recorded makes a new merge of that column and C. Merging an unsaved added column with a saved one sends one source, so the engine records "replaced", not "merged" (the added column never existed). A split of an unsaved merge or split part uses the same saved sources (it was refused before); splitting an unsaved merge gives both parts the merge's sources, which the engine records as two splits and two merges rather than one change (one change would need an engine rule, not built).
5. **"Made from" names old columns the way the engine does** (from the split, replaced and merged amendments), and also from the names seen during the page load (kept by the page, so they survive closing and reopening the panel; review, finding 2), so a split or merge saved before any value names the old columns instead of their ids. This changes the split's display in that case too (it showed ids).
6. **No new change words**: the amendment line uses the existing "columns merged" words ("columns merged, from “Images, Data split” to “Images and Data split”.").
7. **The browser test of Attach and Open in the one-study view** checks the request the new tab makes for the attachment route, not what the tab shows (headless Chromium leaves its address empty for a PDF); it waits for the screen's focus after the load before acting (the race fixed on PR 280).

**Lane STANDARD** (`test_gate_policy.md`: a bounded UI behaviour change in one repository: it writes form amendments through the existing API; no new route, persisted record, refusal code or engine change).

## 2026-11-22 — M11b part C: the screening screens, the build's choices (items 1 to 17 accepted by the researcher 2026-11-22 as built; M11b-criteria-pilot, part C)

Record: `docs/roadmap/milestones/M11b-criteria-pilot.md`, "Build record", "Part C". Choices spec 13 (FINAL 2026-11-21) leaves open, or where the build departs from its words. None is accepted until the researcher answers.

1. **"Needs you" lists the records shown again first, and every queue item carries `shown_again`** (accepted 2026-11-22). Spec 13 question 6 ("first in Needs you, tagged") needs the engine to say which items are shown again; the queue answer gains the additive field `shown_again` (screening API contract §4 note). The order is the engine's.
2. **Items of `view=pilot` carry `pilot_decision`** (accepted 2026-11-22): your pilot decision on the record (id, outcome, reason code, answers, criteria version) or null, so Triage can show it and a change of mind can name it in `supersedes`. Only the pilot view carries it; no main view does (answer 11).
3. **In the pilot view, a decided pilot record shows "Your pilot decision" with Next and Change; Change is offered only while the pilot is open** (accepted 2026-11-22). There is no Undo in the pilot view: a change of mind is Change, which supersedes.
4. **When every pilot record is decided, the pilot's hand-off replaces the record view** (accepted 2026-11-22), as 03's all-decided card does, with "See the pilot result" (brand) and "Screen the rest".
5. **The "Shown again" and "Pilot" options appear in Triage's view select only when they hold something** (accepted 2026-11-22): records waiting to be decided again, or an open pilot. `?view=` opens Triage on a view, so the Prepare card and the result link straight to it.
6. **A `REASON_CODE_RETIRED` refusal reads the record and the criteria again** (accepted 2026-11-22), so the chips are the criteria in force (spec 13, B). The card uses the state copy ("That reason was replaced"); it does not name the id and version, which the refusal does not carry as fields.
7. **The result's words live in `components/screening-pilot-copy.ts`, beside the component** (accepted 2026-11-22; the spec names `screening-pilot.tsx`). Keys the spec does not name are marked "Added" in the file: the "mixed" source of "read differently", "Retired" with no version, a label naming where a reading's answers come from, a line per reading whose minimum count is not reached (the engine's `have` and `min`), the readings' names, "cost not estimated", the confirmation of an addition, a removal and a reason-category change, an unpriced model's estimate and run button, and the "check done" line.
8. **"Retired in version {v}" takes the version from the revision that retired the id** (accepted 2026-11-22), read from `GET /criteria`; when none is listed, the row says "Retired".
9. **The line "No agent drafts for these records. Run the agent to compare." is not shown** (accepted 2026-11-22): the "not computed" line already offers the agent check on these records, with its estimate.
10. **The agent-check dialog** (accepted 2026-11-22): the provider is the last run's, else Anthropic; the model select lists the run screen's three Anthropic models, starting on the last model, else Haiku (spec 13, (i)); the request always carries `model` and `pilot_id`. The calibration route takes no consent, so without consent on file the dialog shows `CONSENT_REQUIRED` and cannot run (consent is given on the run screen). The check is not offered without a provider key; the "not computed" line then has no link.
11. **"Pilot {n} more records" reuses Prepare's draw control** (accepted 2026-11-22), the size field and its confirmation, as a secondary action under the action bar once the pilot is complete or ended; a draw opens Triage on the new pilot.
12. **`Ctrl+Enter` follows the screen's primary link** (accepted 2026-11-22): Continue the pilot while it is open, else Screen the rest.
13. **A second pilot shows the first pilot's "Reason for exclusions" column and one line per count tile** (accepted 2026-11-22); the checklist and agent columns are the newer pilot's only.
14. **The read-only record of the result is a dialog** (accepted 2026-11-22) with the title, your pilot decision (outcome, reason code, criteria version) and the abstract from `GET /records/{id}`; no checklist answers (part B item 10: the result carries none), no decision bar, no draft.
15. **"Draft again" on the run screen** (accepted 2026-11-22): when the setup counts drafts made under an earlier wording, a secondary button opens the run screen's own confirmation for a run scoped to those records (`scope.study_ids`), with its own estimate and the same consent and key gates.
16. **Not built in part C** (accepted 2026-11-22 as follow-ups): the result's offline state (the last result "as of {time}"; offline shows the `ENGINE_UNREACHABLE` card), any phone layout (every pilot route is desktop only, answer 8), and a next-step bar sentence for an open pilot (the bar is unchanged).
17. **Once batches exist, Prepare's one brand action is the pilot card's** (accepted 2026-11-22; review round 1, finding 7): "Draw a pilot of {n}" before a pilot, "Continue the pilot" while one is open, "See the result" after; Prepare's own "Prepare batches" becomes secondary, since preparing again is not the next step once batches exist. Before batches, "Prepare batches" stays the brand button. Spec 13 question 7 settled the card's button, not Prepare's own.

## 2026-11-21 — H-pdf-resolver-locations: the open-access resolver reads every listed location (items 1 to 12 answered by the researcher 2026-11-21: ten accepted as built, item 3 "Three addresses", item 4 changed to "Always ask both services"; items 13 and 14 accepted as built 2026-11-22)

Record: `docs/roadmap/milestones/H-pdf-resolver-locations.md`. Found by the live check after M12a K0: `scholar-pdf download` read only the "best" location of a paper, which is often a landing page, so a paper whose PDF address is in OpenAlex `locations` or Unpaywall `oa_locations` resolved nothing; and a failed lookup read the same as "no open copy". The researcher asked for the fix on 2026-11-21, ahead of Part K. Already decided and applied, not asked again: the best open copy, published version first, then accepted manuscript, then submitted version or preprint, each labelled (M12a answer F3); no guessed address (items 24 and 27); no proxy (item 25); the contact e-mail required. Items 1 to 12 are the build's own choices where the brief left the detail open.
