The researcher answered them in the main session on 2026-11-21; the answers reached this build through the main session's message: item 4 "Always ask both services" (not as built), item 3 "Three addresses", the other ten "Accept as built", subject to the independent review. Item 13 follows from the changed item 4 and item 14 from the independent review's repairs; neither is answered. Nothing here changes a Contract v1 file or `protocol.json`.

1. **Order within one version** (accepted as built): OpenAlex's locations before Unpaywall's, and within a provider its own order (`best_oa_location`, `primary_location`, then the list as given). An address listed twice is kept once, at its first place. *Alternative:* repositories before publishers, or by licence.
2. **A copy with no version label is tried last and labelled `unknown`** (accepted as built), also when the provider gives the version in other words than `publishedVersion`, `acceptedVersion` or `submittedVersion`. *Alternative:* leave such a copy out.
3. **At most three addresses are tried for one DOI** (answered "Three addresses"), the three best-ranked across both lists. Until Part K stops the retries of 403, 404 and 410 (PDF-06), an address that refuses is requested three times, so one DOI costs nine PDF requests at worst. *Alternative:* two addresses; or every listed one.
4. **Decided 2026-11-21: always ask both services (changed from the build's first proposal).** OpenAlex and Unpaywall are each asked once for every DOI, and the best copy is chosen across both lists by F3, so a published version that only Unpaywall lists is taken over a submitted one OpenAlex lists, and an address only Unpaywall lists is tried after OpenAlex's fail (within item 3's three). One more request per DOI where OpenAlex already listed an address: two lookups for every DOI, so 886 for 443 records. `ingest` is not changed: it needs the title, author and year only, and still asks Unpaywall only when OpenAlex gave no answer. *Was proposed:* ask Unpaywall only when OpenAlex lists no open copy with a direct address (the requests as before the fix; a better version listed only by Unpaywall would have been missed).
5. **Which locations count** (accepted as built). OpenAlex: `is_oa: true` on the location itself, for `best_oa_location` and `primary_location` too, so a closed location that carries a `pdf_url` is no longer requested (the location half of Part K item 7, PDF-12, done here). Unpaywall: listed in `best_oa_location` or `oa_locations` and not saying `is_oa: false`; `oa_locations_embargoed` and `first_oa_location` are not read. The address must start with `http://` or `https://`. *Alternative:* trust `best_oa_location` without its `is_oa` field.
6. **A 404 from a service is an answer** ("does not know this DOI"), not a failed lookup (accepted as built). Every other error is a failed lookup: 429, 5xx, a timeout, a network error, another 4xx, a body that is not a JSON object. *Alternative:* a 404 as a failure.
7. **One lookup failed and the other lists no address: the outcome is the failed lookup** ("the answer is incomplete; run again later"), because the service that failed may list a copy (accepted as built). When the other service lists a copy, the download goes ahead and the failure stays on the result. With item 4 as decided this case is more common: both services are asked every time, so one of them failing while the other lists a copy now happens for any DOI, and it never stops the download. Item 13 says what is shown when that copy then gives no PDF. *Alternative:* report "no open copy" and mention the failure.
8. **The three messages** (accepted as built; each ends "This is not a paywall determination."; the part after the colon names each service asked and what it said):
   - "Lookup failed, so the answer is incomplete: OpenAlex failed (HTTP 503); Unpaywall lists no open copy. Run the command again later."
   - "Open access, but no direct PDF address is listed: OpenAlex lists it as open access without a direct PDF address; … No address is guessed; get the PDF from <the open landing page> yourself and use 'scholar-pdf ingest'." The landing page is named, never requested.
   - "No open copy is listed: OpenAlex lists no open copy; Unpaywall does not know this DOI."
   A failure reads "failed (HTTP <status>)", "failed (timed out)" or "failed (<the error's class name>)"; the error's own text is never shown or kept, because it can hold the request address with the contact e-mail.
9. **Exit codes of `download`** (accepted as built): 0 every DOI has its file; 3 a lookup failed; 1 a listed address gave no PDF, or another error (unchanged); 4 open access but no direct PDF address; 5 no open copy listed. With several DOIs the code is the first of 3, 1, 4, 5 that any DOI met. 2 is left to usage errors. Part K's "honest commands" (item 10) may revise them. *Alternative:* 1 for every failure, the reason in words only.
10. **What the result carries** (accepted as built; `DownloadResult`, for Part F to label and export): `outcome` (`ACQUIRED`, `PDF_NOT_FETCHED`, `LOOKUP_FAILED`, `OPEN_NO_DIRECT_PDF`, `NO_OPEN_COPY_LISTED`), `open_copy` (address, version, licence, host type, source name, provider), `lookups` (per service: answered, not known or failed, with the HTTP status or error kind), `copies_listed`, `copies_tried`. `status` keeps its three values. Host type is `publisher` or `repository`: OpenAlex's source type `repository` is a repository and any other type a publisher; Unpaywall's `host_type` as given. Source name is OpenAlex's source name, or for Unpaywall the repository's institution, or the journal for a publisher's copy.
11. **`ingest` says when a metadata lookup failed** (accepted as built): one line; it still stores the file and still exits 0; its exit codes wait for Part K (PDF-15).
12. **`access_status` is left as it is** (accepted as built), except that a file taken from a location its provider marks open reads `VERIFIED_OPEN_ACCESS`. The old rule that a `pdf_url` on a closed "best" or "primary" location reads as open stays until Part K item 7, so for such a paper the result reads `NO_OPEN_COPY_LISTED` with `VERIFIED_OPEN_ACCESS`. *Alternative:* repair the status here (wider than asked).
13. **Accepted by the researcher 2026-11-22 (from item 4 as decided): a listed address gave no PDF while one service had failed.** The outcome stays "a listed address gave no PDF" (`PDF_NOT_FETCHED`, exit code 1), and the message adds which service failed: "… (2 of 2 listed addresses tried); Unpaywall failed (HTTP 503), so its list was not read". *Alternative:* the failed-lookup outcome and exit code 3 ("run again later"), since the service that failed may list another address.
14. **Accepted by the researcher 2026-11-22 (from the review, round 1): two small changes to items accepted as built.** (a) Item 8's failure words gain two forms, because the class name or a status of 200 told the researcher little: "failed (network error)" when the lookup client reports an error with no HTTP status, and "failed (unreadable answer)" for a 200 whose body is not a JSON object; "failed (HTTP <status>)" and "failed (timed out)" are unchanged, and the stored `lookups` still hold the status and the class name. (b) Item 10's `open_copy` is left empty when the file came through the parked gateway (a caller passing one explicitly; the commands refuse a proxy), so those bytes are not labelled with the open location's version and licence. *Alternative:* (a) the class name as accepted; (b) keep the label, or wait for Part K to record that route as institutional access (item 25).

## 2026-11-21 — M11b part D: step 5's method questions and wording checks, the build's choices (items 1 to 20 answered by the researcher 2026-11-22: nineteen accepted, item 5 changed to "Shown, but I must confirm it"; items 21 to 24 accepted as built by the researcher 2026-11-22; M11b-criteria-pilot, part D)

Record: `docs/roadmap/milestones/M11b-criteria-pilot.md`, "Build record", "Part D". Answers 8 (no default for the not-reported rule) and 12 (the terminal wizard asks too) bind the rest. The screen (DoD 20 and the copy) follows `11-criteria-block.md`, FINAL since its questions 3, 7 and 8 were answered (2026-11-21). Items 1 to 20 carry the researcher's answers of 2026-11-22; items 21 to 24 are the build's proposals.

1. *(Accepted 2026-11-22, "Accept as built".)* **A new draft stores `method_blinding: "reveal_after_decision"` and `method_not_reported: null`.** The pre-selection of draft visibility is the record's proposal, not asked separately; the rule is unchosen (answer 8).
2. *(Accepted 2026-11-22, "Accept as built".)* **A save that does not name the two fields keeps the stored values; an explicit `null` after a choice is refused `DRAFT_INVALID`.** Today's step-5 screen sends no method fields, so without the first half every save would drop the choice. *Alternative:* a separate route for the two questions.
3. *(Accepted 2026-11-22, "Accept as built".)* **"Started before this question" means the stored draft has no `method_not_reported` key** (a draft from before part D); the blocker then carries `started_before_question: true` and its longer sentence.
4. *(Accepted 2026-11-22, "Accept as built".)* **The intent carries `method: {blinding, not_reported}`; the protocol kit's `IntentPacket` gains an optional `method`, which the compiler copies** into the protocol's `method` block; `compile_intent` still adds the guideline the playbook implies. An intent without it compiles as before, so no existing protocol's fingerprint moves, and "unclear" with "after you decide" gives the same bytes as the defaults (tested). Not a Contract v1 change (the protocol kit is outside the baseline; `generate_protocol_schema.py --check` unchanged, the intent has no generated schema).
5. *(Changed by the researcher 2026-11-22: "Shown, but I must confirm it". Protocols signed since 2026-11-08 had `unclear` written without anyone choosing it, so carrying it as chosen would show a rule the researcher never chose, against answer 8. Built as items 21 to 24.)* **An imported protocol's `method` block fills both questions** (carried, "From the imported protocol"); its other settings (dedup, the rule table, the retrieval cap) are not carried, and the "method not carried" line appears only when one of them differs from today's defaults.
6. *(Accepted 2026-11-22, "Accept as built".)* **The terminal wizard asks the rule after the criteria are accepted**, with no default, again until one of the three is chosen. A run is "scripted" when standard input is not a terminal or `--auto-select` / `--direction-id` is given; it is refused (exit 2) before anything is asked or written without `--not-reported`. The option is on `inception` and on `init` (which runs the same wizard).
7. *(Accepted 2026-11-22, "Accept as built".)* **A sixth check id, `CRITERIA_WORDING_NOT_CHECKED` (note)**, says that the three English-only checks did not run for criteria in another language, as record (f) asks the panel to say.
8. *(Accepted 2026-11-22, "Accept, with the weak spot"; a French criterion holding "a" read as English is a follow-up.)* **"In English" is read from the criteria themselves:** English when any criterion holds an English function word; otherwise not English when one holds a function word of another language (a short French, German or Spanish list) or a letter outside ASCII; otherwise English. Short criteria such as "UAV imagery; field trials" are read as English. *Alternative:* the review's search languages (they say what papers are searched, not what the criteria are written in).
9. *(Accepted 2026-11-22, "Accept as built".)* **The word rules' details:** stop words and negation words are fixed English lists (negations: not, no, without, only, non, except, excluding, and the phrase "other than"; "other" alone is a content word, review D round 1, finding 8); the mirror share is the larger of the two coverages; a criterion equal to another is a duplicate or a contradiction, never a mirror; a full-text phrase matches whole words only ("table" does not match "tabletop").
10. *(Accepted 2026-11-22, "Accept as built".)* **A pair's subject is "id|id"**, so each pair is dismissed alone; criterion ids come from the text, so a reworded criterion is a new subject and a dismissal does not carry over.
11. *(Accepted 2026-11-22, "Accept".)* **Continue opens as soon as the rule is chosen in the tab** (spec 11 question 8 left it open): `steps/criteria` answers for the draft as typed, and Continue saves before it moves on, so the saved draft always carries the choice. The checks panel still describes the saved draft until the save.
12. *(Accepted 2026-11-22, "Accept as built".)* **The criteria section does not fold**, as the concepts block of M11a does not: its header reads "{n} inclusion, {m} exclusion criteria" when nothing waits, else "Decide every criterion first." The "one block open" rule, the written state with Edit, and the "See the check below" chip on a row named by a check are not built.
13. *(Accepted 2026-11-22, "Accept as built".)* **The check rows stay in step 5's checks panel**, below the page as today, not inside the criteria section; their links focus the named row, the out-of-scope field or the rule's first option.
14. *(Accepted 2026-11-22, "Accept as built".)* **The out-of-scope field holds the whole list**: "Add an out-of-scope topic" writes the field to the page's draft (semicolons and line breaks separate items, commas do not), and each item becomes an exclusion row waiting for Accept. Shape shows one line ("Out of scope is asked with the criteria.") and keeps the stored items as they are; the keys `shape.outOfScope` and `shape.outOfScopeHelp` are removed, a proposal card names the field with `field.out_of_scope`.
15. *(Accepted 2026-11-22, "Accept as built".)* **A save writes the visibility the screen shows**, also for a draft that never stored it (the engine's `method.blinding`), as spec 11's Data out asks.
16. *(Accepted 2026-11-22, "Accept as built".)* **The rule's group says "Not chosen yet" from the engine's `method.chosen`**, with "This draft was started before this question existed." when the engine says so; Continue is held and names why. The group carries no `aria-required` (not an allowed attribute on a group; axe refuses it) and is described by that line; `aria-invalid` after a refused Continue is not built because Continue cannot be pressed while the rule is unchosen.
17. *(Answered 2026-11-22: "Follow-ups".)* **Not built in part D**: the J, K, A, R keys and Ctrl+Enter of spec 11 (step 5's keys are unchanged); the older-engine sentence (`criteria.method.err.engine`; one package, so this screen never meets an older engine); the narrow layout's collapsible sections.
18. *(Accepted 2026-11-22, "Accept as built".)* **"From the imported protocol"** shows under the questions when the import report says it carried `method_not_reported`. Item 24 replaces this rule: the label now follows the engine's `source`.
19. *(Accepted 2026-11-22, "Accept as built".)* **The check copy uses spec 11's words**, which differ slightly from the engine sentence in `02-checks` ("what the agent should do" for "what the agent's draft does"); the `{side}` slot of `CRITERIA_DUPLICATE` is translated through the panel's `checks.side.*` words.
20. *(Accepted 2026-11-22, "Accept as built".)* **The guidance audit**: row I-SH10 now names the moved line (STAYS); rows I-C24 to I-C30 and I-R22 add the new keys, all STAYS (the measured figures and the limits line are evidence, kept as text); the counts table reads 116 STAYS, 34 HINT, 11 REMOVED, 161 rows for Inception.
21. **An imported rule is shown but not chosen** (accepted as built 2026-11-22; item 5 as changed): the importer fills `method_not_reported` and marks it `method_not_reported_source: "imported"`; `method_choice` answers `chosen: false` and the `source`, so step 5, Review and genesis stay blocked by `METHOD_RULE_UNCHOSEN` with its own sentence ("The imported protocol's rule is shown: confirm it or choose another.", key `METHOD_RULE_UNCHOSEN.imported`) and a preview or genesis refusal carries `details.imported`. No new refusal code.
22. **The engine needs one new draft field, `method_not_reported_source`** (accepted as built 2026-11-22): `imported` (shown, blocking), `confirmed` (the imported rule kept by the researcher, which records the confirmation), `chosen` (one the researcher picked), or null (a rule chosen before the field existed, which counts as chosen). A save that does not name it keeps it; a confirmed or picked rule cannot be marked imported again (`DRAFT_INVALID`); a save whose value differs from the stored imported one becomes `chosen`, whatever source it sends. The intent and the protocol do not carry the source (no change to `IntentPacket`, no fingerprint change); it stays in the draft record.
23. **The draft-visibility question is carried as it is on import** (accepted as built 2026-11-22): it has a pre-selection the researcher did not question (item 1) and its value is shown on Review before signing, so it needs no confirmation.
24. **The screen** (accepted as built 2026-11-22): an imported rule shows pre-selected with "From the imported protocol" and, in the group, "From the imported protocol: confirm this rule or choose another." with a "Keep this rule" button; keeping it saves `confirmed` (the label stays), choosing another option saves `chosen` and drops the label, both from the engine's `source`. Review adds "From the imported protocol; not confirmed yet." under an unconfirmed rule. The terminal wizard has no path that starts from an imported protocol (it always asks the rule), so it needs no change.

## 2026-11-21 — M11b part B: the pilot, the build's choices (items 1 to 13 accepted by the researcher 2026-11-21; M11b-criteria-pilot, part B)

Record: `docs/roadmap/milestones/M11b-criteria-pilot.md`, "Build record", "Part B". Choices the specification leaves open (answers 5, 6, 9 and 11 of 2026-11-21 bind the rest). The researcher answered them in the main session on 2026-11-21: item 11 "Accept as built"; item 4 "Accept"; item 6 (with spec 13 question 8, the phone) "Desktop only for now" (a read-only result on the phone may come later); items 1, 2, 3, 5, 7, 8, 9, 10, 12 and 13 "Accept as built" (asked together).

1. **A pilot decision's id is derived, so a replay is a duplicate.** (accepted as built) The client mints an `event_id` (a UUID, as for a decision event); the engine's `pilot_decision_id` is `PLD-` + the first 24 hex of sha256(pilot id, event id). A replay of a stored decision answers `duplicate`, also after the pilot ended or a criterion was retired; the same id with other content is refused `IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT`. *Alternative:* an engine-minted random id (no idempotency for an offline retry).
2. **Two refusal codes beyond the specification's three:** (accepted as built) `PILOT_NOT_FOUND` (404, an unknown pilot id; spec 13 question 1 left the code to the engine) and `PILOT_SIZE_INVALID` (422: `n` outside 20 to 100 in tens, more than the records left, or no `confirm`). The draw reuses `BATCHES_NOT_PREPARED` before batches and `CRITERIA_AMENDMENT_AFTER_APPROVAL` after an approval (one rule for the draw and the revision).
3. **A pilot's end is its own file** (accepted as built), `pilot_NNN_closed.json` (reason `ended` or `complete`), so the draw file is never rewritten. The decision that decides the last record writes it (`complete`); "End the pilot" writes it with `ended`.
4. **Minimum counts for the readings that the record states as shares only:** (accepted) "many Unclear" and "never used" are read from 10 pilot decisions on; "does most of the work" from 5 exclusions. The other minimums are the record's (10 records for a mirror, a hard-to-judge or a read-differently; 5 for exclusions that fire together). *Why:* in a pilot just begun, one Unclear of two is "50%".
5. **"Read differently" compares yes and no only.** (accepted as built) A `not_reported` on either side, or an Unclear (which implies nothing), is not compared; the denominator is the records where both sides said yes or no. The source is `answers`, `implied` or `mixed` per criterion.
6. **Every pilot route is desktop-only, the result included.** (accepted: desktop only for now) Spec 13 question 8 suggests a read-only result on the phone; the engine keeps it on the desktop until part C settles that screen.
7. **The open pilot's records leave every main queue view, but `/summary.needs_you` still counts them** (accepted as built) (they are undecided). The pilot block of `/summary` says how many are in the pilot.
8. **The methods sentence counts the pilot records decided, not drawn** (accepted as built), and names each pilot's seed ("piloted on 38 records (seed …)"), so a pilot ended early is not overstated; the drawn count is in the figures. An amendment made with no counted decision reads "It was made during the pilot, before the main screening." (the amendment row names the open pilot) or "… after the pilot, …".
9. **The agent check on a pilot compares a draft with a pilot decision only when both were made under the same criteria version** (accepted as built), as the main calibration does since part A.
10. **The agent check's drafts and runs name the pilot** (accepted as built) (`pilot_id` on the run record and on each draft), so the pilot result can tell them apart later; nothing reads it for counting today.
11. **A pilot decision is labelled with the criteria version in force when it is received** (accepted as built) (no binding: it is part of no batch). Since part A's item 5 was decided ("bind the version"), a main decision made under an earlier wording is refused; a pilot decision made just before a revision is not refused, it is labelled with the new version. It is never counted, so only the pilot result's per-version labels can be off by the requests in flight at the moment of a revision. *Alternative:* the pilot queue hands out the version, and the pilot intake refuses a decision bound under another (as part A item 5); the same few lines of code, one more refusal for Triage in pilot mode (part C).
12. **A second pilot decision on a record must supersede the first** (accepted as built) (review round 1, F7): refused `INVALID_OUTPUT` otherwise, so two tabs cannot both decide a record with the last one silently winning. A change of mind names the decision it replaces (section (d)).
13. **A pilot is not drawn while a batch was decided through the file hand-off** (accepted as built) (review round 1, F6): refused `CRITERIA_AMENDMENT_HANDOFF`, the code part A gives a revision in the same state (part A item 10), because "no main decision" cannot be read for records decided outside the event log.

## 2026-11-21 — M12a Part B: the charting form, cells, pilot and amendments (items 1 to 34 accepted by the researcher 2026-11-22 as built)

Record: `docs/roadmap/milestones/M12a-charting-table.md`, "Build record", Part B. The build's own choices where the specification left the detail open; none is accepted until the researcher answers. Nothing here changes a Contract v1 file or `protocol.json`.

1. **The form is seeded at the table's first read** (`GET /api/v1/charting` or `/form`), from `matrix_dimensions` in order: `free_text` → Text, `numeric` → Number, `categorical` → One of a list, `list` → Several of a list, with no options (the protocol holds none); Yes/No exists only in the form, its options fixed to Yes and No. A column id is the protocol's id made safe. While the table is read-only (protocol replaced) nothing is seeded.
2. **One route for every form change: `PUT /api/v1/charting/form` with the version it was made from** (the amendment rows, the form and its journal row are written together or not at all, `ATOMIC_COMMIT_FAILED` otherwise; "has values" is read while no cell can be stored), instead of spec 01's separate `POST …/form/amendments`: before any value it saves at once (`CHARTING_FORM_CHANGED`, a journal action of its own); after, the same call needs the one-sentence reason and becomes the amendment. A change made from an old version is refused `CHARTING_FORM_STALE` (409, a new code).
3. **What an amendment row is**: one row per change in `charting/form_amendments.jsonl`, sharing one amendment id: added, removed, renamed, definition, type, options added, options removed, required, order, split, merged, replaced; with from, to, the reason, pilot on or not, who, when, the form versions, the protocol fingerprint and the generation id.
4. **A split or a merge is said by the new columns**: a new column names the column(s) it comes from (`derived_from`); one old column into several is a split, several into one a merge, one into one a replacement. The old column's cells stay in the log. The new columns keep naming their sources in later saves (the split's history is read from them); only a column new in a save, or whose sources change, must name columns of the current form. A `form.json` this version cannot read is refused `CHARTING_FORM_STALE` and kept, never seeded over.
5. **What "Chart again" marks**: a cell saved before a type change or a replacement of its column; a cell whose value uses an option removed after it was saved (a cell using kept options is not marked); an empty cell of a column made by a split or merge when the old column held something for that study (its value shown as history). A rename, a new definition, an added option, a required change or a reorder marks nothing. Charting the cell again clears the mark; the history keeps the last five earlier values.
6. **Cell values**: Text is non-empty, at most 5,000 characters; Number is a JSON number (no units; a unit belongs in the column's name or the note); One of and Yes/No one option; Several of a non-empty list of distinct options. Not reported and Not applicable hold no value. A note (2,000) and a where-from (500) are optional on any cell.
7. **A cell's undo chain is its column**: an undo names an earlier cell event of the same study and column.
8. **The pilot**: each cell event records whether the pilot was on when it was saved; "End pilot" is recorded once (`CHARTING_PILOT_ENDED`); values charted in the pilot stay final (spec 01 question 6, "Kept as final"). The pilot is not restarted.
9. **"Charted"**: a kept row not excluded at full text whose every column holds a value, Not reported or Not applicable, and none marked Chart again. Counts gain charted, not charted (rows excluded at full text in neither) and pilot (rows with a pilot value).
10. **Cells on a row excluded at full text are not refused by the engine**; the screens make them read-only (spec 02, "Excluded at full text").
11. **The Charting CSV** (`view=charting`): study id, first author, year, title, DOI, round, full-text status, "No full text", "Pilot", then per column its value, note and where-from; Not reported and Not applicable as words, a cell not charted empty, a "several" value joined with "; "; rows excluded at full text and rows no longer kept left out; `CHARTING_EXPORTED` with the SHA-256 and the not-charted count.
12. **`GET /api/v1/charting/records/{study_id}`** answers one row with the form for the one-study view. It reads the whole table: the projection groups the events once per answer (review of Part B, finding 5), about 0.01 s for 150 rows × 5 columns with 1,800 events on this machine (it was 0.35 s and grew with the square of the events before).
13. **The one-study view is `/[locale]/charting/study?id=STU-…`**, a query parameter rather than spec 02's path segment: the static export cannot prerender a page per study, and the app's other dynamic screens avoid it the same way. Back still works (item 14). *This changes spec 02's closed question 1 ("a path, so Back works", closed 2026-11-21); accepted by the researcher 2026-11-22 ("Accept the ?id= address").*
14. **The table's place lives in the address**: the table writes `view`, `show` and `excluded` (replacing the address, not adding history) and reads `row` and `form=open` once; Esc and "Back to the table" return to the same view, filter and row. In the one-study view J and K replace the address, so the browser's Back goes to the table, not through every study.
15. **The one-study view's neighbours** are the table's Show list as it was when the study was opened, kept fixed; if the study is not in it, all rows. "Study k of n" counts in that list.
16. **Keys in the Charting view**: arrows (inside the grid only; left and right mirror in Arabic), J/K, Enter, Alt+N, Alt+A (read from the physical key, so they work on macOS), 1, 2, F, O, Z, Ctrl+E and ?; the Full text keys (G Q R N I E P X A, Shift+J/K) do nothing there. L, C and D stay free for Part F.
17. **Alt+N and Alt+A toggle**: the same key again clears the cell; Z undoes that.
18. **In-cell editors**: Text and Number on one line; One of and Yes/No as radios; Several of as checkboxes; Enter saves and moves down, Esc cancels, leaving the editor saves, a double-click opens it; an unchanged value sends nothing (a "Chart again" cell confirmed as it is does send).
19. **A number the screen cannot read for sure is not sent**: the cell says "Type a number, for example 1260 or 2.5." (a new key). "1 260" is read as 1260, "2,5" and "2.5" as 2.5; a comma before exactly three digits ("1,260": a thousands separator or a decimal comma), two commas, or a hexadecimal is asked again rather than guessed (review of Part B, finding 3: "1,260" had been stored as 1.26).
20. **Notes and where-from are edited in the one-study view only**; a value saved from the table keeps the cell's note and where-from; on an empty cell they wait for a value (the engine keeps no note on a cleared cell).
21. **Enter in a one-study field** saves it and moves to the next column's value; Shift+Enter in Note adds a line.
22. **The toolbar's Pilot switch only turns off** (the same recorded "End pilot" as the panel's button) and is then disabled: the engine has no way to restart the pilot (engine item 8). Rows charted in the pilot carry a "Pilot" mark.
23. **The first-visit card** shows while the pilot is on, no value exists, the form has columns and the table is not read-only; "Not now" closes it for this page load (the app's rule N12 keeps browser storage out of the screens, so it is not remembered across visits); while it shows, its button is the one brand button.
24. **"Chart the next study"** opens the first kept row, not excluded at full text and not charted, in the engine's order (not the filtered list); once none is left it becomes Export and downloads the Charting CSV.
25. **The form panel**: a new column is "New column {n}", Text, saved at once; a column with an empty name is not sent; "Split into two" makes "{name} (1)" and "{name} (2)" from the old column; saves are sent in order; if the engine asks for a reason the screen did not expect, the panel shows the amendment prompt; on `CHARTING_FORM_STALE` the table is read again. Merging columns in the panel is not built (the engine supports it).
26. **The amendments list**: grouped by amendment, newest first, one sentence per change with its own words (new keys `charting.form.change.*`), the column name for definition, type, options and required changes, the reason under each group; "who" is the screener id as stored.
27. **Export is a small menu**: CSV of the Full text view and CSV of the Charting view (no xlsx until Part C); Ctrl+E opens it; after the Charting CSV the status line adds "{n} cells are not charted yet.", counted on the screen.
28. **"No full text" in the table** shows only on rows with at least one charted cell; in the one-study view, whenever there is no full text.
29. **The one-study view's full-text block**: the status as a group of four radio buttons; the decision as Include / Exclude toggle buttons; on a study excluded at full text the focus goes to Include.
30. **Phone cards are links to the one-study view and list the charted values.** *This changes Part A's item 29 ("On a phone the cards are not links (the one-study view comes with Part B)"), accepted 2026-11-21; the change was accepted by the researcher 2026-11-22 ("Accept: cards are links").*
31. **Undo is per page load**, in the table and in the one-study view separately; it does not carry between the two. Part A's item 18 says "per browser session"; Part A's screen already worked per page load. *A reading of an accepted item; accepted by the researcher 2026-11-22 ("Accept: per page load").*
32. **The first accepted cell reads the table once more**, so "has values" (which makes form changes amendments) comes from the engine, not from the screen.
33. **The one-study view is a focus screen**: from 1,024 px its own bar replaces the site header, as triage's does.
34. **The one-study route answers the screener id too** (`GET /api/v1/charting/records/{study_id}`), so the one-study view does not need the table's answer for it; it still reads the table once for its neighbours.

**Lane RELEASE** (new persisted records `charting/form.json` and `charting/form_amendments.jsonl`, new routes and refusal codes).

## 2026-11-21 — M12a Part A: rows, full-text status and decision, attachments (items 1 to 36 accepted by the researcher 2026-11-21 as built; items 2 and 8 as reworded after the review, items 34 to 36 as added by it)

Record: `docs/roadmap/milestones/M12a-charting-table.md`, "Build record", Part A. The build's own choices where the specification left the detail open. The researcher answered items 1 to 33 on 2026-11-21 (in the main session, subject to the review then running): item 1 "Accept: readable, marked stale", item 9 "Accept: only a PDF does", item 14 "Accept the gap", the others "Accept as built"; wording and layout stay open until the researcher sees the built screen. The review's round 2 (APPROVE) changed the meaning of items 2 and 8 and added items 34 to 36; those go back to the researcher. Nothing here changes a Contract v1 file or `protocol.json` (decision item 1 of the M12a entry: outside the contract).

1. *(Accepted 2026-11-21, "Accept: readable, marked stale". The checksum rule came with the review of Part A, finding 1, and was in the question the researcher answered.)* **What "published" means for the table.** The current round's publication is read when its status is CURRENT or STALE (decisions changed after publishing: the table follows the published files and says so, spec 01 "Stale"). In every case each round's `included.json` must have the SHA-256 its own `generation.json` recorded, so a file changed after publishing is never read (review of Part A, finding 1: STALE also covers an output that no longer matches its digest, and archived rounds were not checked). Any other status, or a file that does not match, refuses `SCREENING_NOT_PUBLISHED`, except while a citation-chasing round is being screened: the earlier rounds stay readable and the table says a round is in progress. *Alternative:* CURRENT only.
2. *(Accepted 2026-11-21; reworded after the review and accepted again the same day, "Accept as built".)* **`charting/kept_history.jsonl` remembers each published kept set.** A re-publication overwrites `included.json`, so the table writes one row per round and generation the first time it reads it (under a lock; not journaled: it repeats what the publication already recorded). *Reworded after the review:* when that lock cannot be taken the table reads the history without writing to it, so that publication is recorded on the next visit (item 36). "No longer kept" and "New since <date>" come from it; the first visit marks nothing new. *Alternative:* keep a copy of each published `included.json`.
3. *(Accepted 2026-11-21, "Accept as built".)* **The study id** is the record's `study_id`, else the published `workspace_id` (`STU-…`), as the collector reads it.
4. *(Accepted 2026-11-21, "Accept as built".)* **Events are recorded for the workspace's screener only** (`literature/screening/settings.json`, default `researcher:local`); another screener id is refused `BODY_INVALID`. The engine stamps `method: "HUMAN"` and the binding (protocol fingerprint, the newest round's generation id and the SHA-256 of its `generation.json`, the round, `form_version: null` until Part B).
5. *(Accepted 2026-11-21, "Accept as built".)* **Undo** names an earlier event of the same study, screener **and kind**; the value of a (study, kind) is the last stored event no later event supersedes.
6. *(Accepted 2026-11-21, "Accept as built".)* **A reason where none is allowed is `FULLTEXT_REASON_INVALID`** (DoD 3 names only `FULLTEXT_REASON_REQUIRED`; spec 01 defines `_INVALID` for a reason that is not an option, and a reason on Retrieved or on an Include is not one).
7. *(Accepted 2026-11-21, "Accept as built".)* **The PDF is the raw request body** (`application/pdf`; the file name and the client's event ids in the query), not multipart: no new dependency. The check by first bytes is a `%PDF-` header in the first 1,024 bytes (the kit's header rule); a retried upload with the same event id and the same bytes is a duplicate, other bytes `IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT`.
8. *(Accepted 2026-11-21; reworded after the review and accepted again the same day, "Accept as built".)* **Nothing is deleted.** A file replaced by another, or taken off by an undo, moves to `pdfs/replaced/<study_id>.<sha256[:16]>.pdf` and comes back when an undo restores it; `pdfs/<study_id>.pdf` holds the file the current attachment event names; *reworded after the review:* after a move that failed once the events were stored, the two can be out of step until the next read of that attachment puts the file in place (item 35); the file served is always the one the event names, by its hash.
9. *(Accepted 2026-11-21, "Accept: only a PDF does".)* **A link does not change the status**; only a PDF turns To get into Retrieved.
10. *(Accepted 2026-11-21, "Accept as built".)* **`ATTACHMENT_MISSING` (404)**, a new code, for opening a record with no PDF attached.
11. *(Accepted 2026-11-21, "Accept as built".)* **`CHARTING_PROTOCOL_REPLACED`** when `protocol.json` differs from the protocol the newest published round was screened under: the table stays readable, writes are refused. A re-inception archives `charting/` with the old protocol (added to the archive list); the archived table's own read-only view is not built (after a re-inception the workspace has no publication, so it shows "not published").
12. *(Accepted 2026-11-21, "Accept as built".)* **The Full text CSV in Part A**: `GET /api/v1/charting/export?format=csv&view=fulltext` (study id, first author, year, title, DOI, round, title-and-abstract decision, status, request date, not-retrieved reason, file name and SHA-256, link, full-text decision and reason; records no longer kept left out), UTF-8 with BOM and CRLF, journaled `CHARTING_EXPORTED` with the file's SHA-256; any other format or view `CHARTING_EXPORT_FORMAT_INVALID` until Parts B and C.
13. *(Accepted 2026-11-21, "Accept as built".)* **The next step after Publish**: state DONE now opens the charting table (Publish's export stays as a second link); PUBLISHED (a chase is possible) offers the table as a second link.
14. *(Accepted 2026-11-21, "Accept the gap".)* **The header's "Charting" item** shows once the next-step answer says screening is published (phase `published`, state `PUBLISHED`, or round above 1), remembered for the browser session, and always on `/charting` itself. The header has no publication read of its own; adding one would change the reads other screens make. *Gap:* on a first visit straight to the workspaces list, the item appears only after a screen has shown the next-step bar. *Alternative:* a publication read in the header.
15. *(Accepted 2026-11-21, "Accept as built".)* **No `g c` shortcut**: the app has no g-prefix shortcuts.
16. *(Accepted 2026-11-21, "Accept as built".)* **No next-step bar on `/charting` itself** (the screen is mapped, the bar is not drawn there).
17. *(Accepted 2026-11-21, "Accept as built".)* **Event ids are UUIDv7** from the app's existing minting (as triage; spec 01 says UUIDv7); the engine accepts any lowercase UUID.
18. *(Accepted 2026-11-21, "Accept as built".)* **A change supersedes the row's current event of that kind; undo supersedes your own event and carries the earlier value.** The undo stack is per browser session; one entry covers a bulk action, or an attachment and its Retrieved status.
19. *(Accepted 2026-11-21, "Accept as built".)* **Bulk targets**: a status key acts on the selection (kept rows only) when there is one, else on the focused row; the confirmation line appears for more than one row only.
20. *(Accepted 2026-11-21, "Accept as built".)* **Not retrieved on several rows** is one line: the bulk sentence and the reason chips; one Enter confirms both.
21. *(Accepted 2026-11-21, "Accept as built".)* **Selection keys**: `A` selects every kept row shown, or clears them when all are selected; `Esc` with nothing open clears the selection; changing Show clears the selection.
22. *(Accepted 2026-11-21, "Accept as built".)* **Rows no longer kept** appear under "All" only; every other filter and every action leaves them out.
23. *(Accepted 2026-11-21, "Accept as built".)* **Requested has no date control in Part A**: `Q` saves Requested without a date; a stored date is shown.
24. *(Accepted 2026-11-21, "Accept as built".)* **Export is one button** (one format in Part A) that downloads the Full text CSV; the status line then says "Exported {when}: {n} rows…".
25. *(Accepted 2026-11-21, "Accept as built".)* **Offline before any change this session** says "The console is not answering. Nothing is lost." (new key); changes are refused locally; Export is off; "Try again" reloads.
26. *(Accepted 2026-11-21, "Accept as built".)* **Dates** ("New since", the changes banner) print as `YYYY-MM-DD` (the app's `localDate`), not "21 Nov".
27. *(Accepted 2026-11-21, "Accept as built".)* **To get has a neutral badge** (outline and an inbox icon; the spec gives it no role); Requested and "To decide" use `unclear`, Retrieved `include`, Not retrieved `blocked`.
28. *(Accepted 2026-11-21, "Accept as built".)* **Two sticky columns** (select and Study); the grid scrolls inside its own box (70 % of the window's height).
29. *(Accepted 2026-11-21, "Accept as built".)* **On a phone the cards are not links** (the one-study view comes with Part B), and keys do nothing below 640 px.
30. *(Accepted 2026-11-21, "Accept as built".)* **Copy keys beyond spec §Copy** (column headers, select labels, the words prompts, confirm and cancel, file labels, help, and refusal keys for `ATTACHMENT_MISSING`, `BODY_INVALID`, `IDEMPOTENCY_CONFLICT`, `ATOMIC_COMMIT_FAILED`, offline and unknown), in en, fr and ar.
31. *(Accepted 2026-11-21, "Accept as built".)* **Names**: the French header item is "Extraction", the Arabic "استخراج البيانات"; Inception's key `criteria.matrixParked` is renamed `criteria.matrixCharting` and loses its dashed "parked" look; Publish reads "Writes the kept studies (Include and Unclear), the excluded records and the … report … Full texts are handled on the Charting step."
32. *(Accepted 2026-11-21, "Accept as built".)* **"Chart the included studies"** sits beside Export and Chase on Home (only while the publication is CURRENT) and beside the "Published" badge on Export.
33. *(Accepted 2026-11-21, "Accept as built".)* **An attachment's answer lists each event with its kind** (`events: [{event_id, kind}]`, beside `event_ids`), so an undo does not rely on the order of the ids.
34. *(Added by the review of Part A, finding 4; accepted by the researcher 2026-11-21.)* **The CSV export is made only from the app's own pages**: `GET /api/v1/charting/export` from another site is refused `CONSOLE_ORIGIN_REFUSED` by the proxy and by the console's browser path, as the agent's draft read is, because an export writes a `CHARTING_EXPORTED` journal row. `GET /api/v1/charting` (which may append to `kept_history.jsonl`, not the journal) is left unguarded; the reviewer noted it as a possible follow-up.
35. *(Added by the review, finding 9; accepted by the researcher 2026-11-21.)* **A PDF that cannot be moved (for example the earlier file is open in a viewer)**: before the events are stored the attachment is refused `ATOMIC_COMMIT_FAILED` (503) and nothing is stored; after they are stored (an undo), the events stand, the move is logged, the next read of that attachment puts the file in place, and meanwhile the file served is found by its hash.
36. *(Added by the review, finding 7; accepted by the researcher 2026-11-21.)* **A busy history lock never fails the table**: `GET /api/v1/charting` reads `kept_history.jsonl` without writing when its lock cannot be taken.

**Lane RELEASE** (new public routes and refusal codes, a new persisted record, a new reader of published artifacts, a change to the same-origin proxy).

## 2026-11-21 — M11b part A: criteria in force, the build's choices (items 1 to 11 answered by the researcher 2026-11-21: ten accepted as built, item 5 changed to "bind the version"; M11b-criteria-pilot, part A)

Record: `docs/roadmap/milestones/M11b-criteria-pilot.md`, "Build record, part A". Choices the specification leaves open, found when the code was read at `dacabd94` (step 0 of the build). The researcher answered them in the main session on 2026-11-21: items 1 and 10 "Accept as built"; item 9 "Accept"; items 2, 3, 4, 6, 7 and 8 "Accept as built" (asked together); item 5 "Bind the version" (the alternative the question recommended), built before part A's PR.

1. **The file hand-off path refuses after a criteria revision** (accepted as built). Batch files embed the signed wording and are hashed into every decision's binding (`screening/batcher.py:76-132`, `:242`), so they cannot be rewritten. The file hand-off worker (`agent_screen.py`, `screening/worker.py:196`) reads the criteria ids and wording from the batch file; once the criteria version is above 1 it refuses (`CRITERIA_AMENDED_USE_ENGINE`) instead of recommending under the old wording and ids. *Alternative:* a second, versioned instructions file beside each batch (more files; a second wording on disk that readers must choose between).
2. **Readers outside DoD 2's list** (accepted as built). The MCP server's screening tools (`scholar_agent/server.py`) read the criteria in force. `prompt-gate measure` and `scholar_agent/calibration.py` stay on the signed list: the record puts `measure` out of scope, and these command-line tools measure the signed protocol. The DoD 2 reader test allow-lists them by name with this reason. *Alternative:* move the command-line tools too.
3. **A draft's id names the criteria version when it is above 1** (accepted as built). `proposal_id = sha256(task_version, input_sha256, protocol_fingerprint, model)` (`screening/proposals.py`); under A1 the fingerprint does not move, so a draft under version 2 would carry the id of the version 1 draft for the same record and model. From version 2 on the version is added to the hashed list; for version 1 the recipe is unchanged, so every existing id stays the same. Amends §2 of `docs/architecture/screening-api-contract.md` (a harness document, not baseline-locked). *Alternative:* keep the recipe and tell drafts apart by `criteria_version` and `created_at` (two drafts would share one id).
4. **A record waiting to be shown again is left out of the batch decisions file** (accepted as built). The file built from events (`events.project_batch`) omits it, so the existing coverage checks refuse approval (`check_exact_coverage`) and collect; Summary and the queue count it as needing a decision. An amendment rebuilds every batch's decisions file under the events lock, so none is left stale (`PROJECTION_STALE`). The file then depends on the event log and the amendments file. `criteria_version` is not added to that file (it feeds the Contract v1 collector); it stays on the event log and `proposals.jsonl`. *Alternative:* keep the earlier decision in the file and add a separate check at approval and at collect (two checks to keep in step; a file that looks complete).
5. **Decided 2026-11-21: bind the version (changed from the build's first proposal).** `GET /records/{id}` hands out a `binding` that names the criteria version the record is shown under (`binding.criteria_version`), and intake refuses a new event bound under another version with `STALE_BINDING` (the message names both versions). A decision made under the earlier wording, for example queued offline on a phone across a revision, is therefore refused and its record stays in the queue to be decided again; it is never stamped with the new version. The engine still stamps `criteria_version` itself; the client only echoes what the engine handed it, as it does for the batch hash; no device clock is trusted. A binding without the field reads as version 1. The check runs on new events only and not in `is_current`, so a stored event replays as a `duplicate` and decisions made under version 1 keep counting. Harness screening API contract §3.1; Contract v1 unchanged. *Was proposed:* stamp at receipt (an offline Include made under the earlier wording would have been counted under the new one without a second look). *Not chosen:* compare `decided_at` with `amended_at` (trusts the device's clock).
6. **A decision on a record shown again must supersede the decision shown again** (accepted as built) (refused `INVALID_OUTPUT` otherwise, no new code). A second head with the same outcome is a confirmation and the earliest head wins (`events.winning_event`), so without the rule the earlier decision, possibly citing a retired id, would stay the published one. *Alternative:* a new refusal code.
7. **A record shown again is also in "needs you"** (accepted as built) (`GET /queue?view=needs_you`, and `needs_you` on `/summary`), besides `view=recheck`; `/summary.decided` does not count it. As the screen draft `13-criteria-pilot.md` question 6 recommends.
8. **The shape of a split** (accepted as built). `text` is one string for reword, merge and add, and a list of at least two strings for split; new criteria of a split or merge keep the first replaced criterion's reason category and research-question links unless `reason_category` is sent.
9. **A later citation-chasing round counts as "after approval"** (accepted): a round after the first starts from a published screening (M3g), so a revision there is refused `CRITERIA_AMENDMENT_AFTER_APPROVAL`, as the record's "In and out" puts revising in a chase round out of scope.

10. **A batch decided through the file hand-off blocks a revision** (review round 1, F1; accepted as built). A worker recommendation, or a decisions file not built from decision events, lives outside the event log, so a revision could neither count those decisions nor show them again, and the batch would be approved under the old wording while the methods paragraph said no record had been decided. While any current batch holds one, a revision (and its dry call) is refused `CRITERIA_AMENDMENT_HANDOFF` (409), a new code. Item 1 already stops the worker after a revision. *Alternative:* let the revision through and refuse approval of such a batch once the criteria version is above 1 (the methods sentence would then need the hand-off decisions counted, which the engine cannot see).
11. **A conflict resolved after a revision keeps the version of the decision it keeps** (review round 3; accepted by the researcher 2026-11-21). With item 5 as decided, the resolve route copied the kept event's binding from before the revision and every such resolution was refused `STALE_BINDING`, leaving the batch blocked with no route out in the app. Now the route binds the resolution to the record as it is now and stamps it with the criteria version of the decision it keeps (an engine-only parameter of intake; a client cannot set it), and that decision may cite a criterion retired since. So a revision that could change the kept decision (any change of wording for an Include or Unclear; a touched criterion for an Exclude) shows the record again in `view=recheck`, where it is decided under the current wording; a revision that could not (a reason category only, an untouched criterion) lets the resolution count. *Alternative:* stamp the resolution with the version in force (the researcher chose a decision made under the earlier wording; it would count under the new one without a second look, which item 5 was decided to prevent). **Accepted by the researcher 2026-11-21** (main session), as built.

Not a choice, recorded for the reviewer: `REASON_CODE_RETIRED` is checked on new events only, as the F7 limits are, so a retry of a stored event stays a `duplicate`.

## 2026-11-21 — M11b criteria pilot: the specification's choices (items 1 to 10 answered by the researcher 2026-11-21: 1 to 4, 7, 9 and 10 as recommended; 5, 6 and 8 against the recommendation; items 11 and 12 answered the same day, both as recommended; the rework of 5, 6 and 8 PROPOSED; M11b-criteria-pilot)

Record: `docs/roadmap/milestones/M11b-criteria-pilot.md`; the researcher's page: `M11b-criteria-pilot-for-review.md`. Screens (drafts): `docs/ux/phase2-screening/final/13-criteria-pilot.md`, `docs/ux/phase0-inception/final/11-criteria-block.md`. Found by the acceptance run of 2026-11-20 (findings 1, 2, 31, 36): criteria written before any record was read, mirrored exclusions that doubled the agent's `not_reported` answers, and the method settings fixed at genesis without a question. Today changing a criterion moves the protocol fingerprint, and every decision, batch and the corpus then stop counting (code at `1f218054`; record, "What exists"). No Contract v1 model, schema, identity or fixture changes; parts A, B and D are RELEASE, C is STANDARD.

1. **A criteria revision is an amendment beside the signed protocol** (accepted, as recommended): one line of `audit/criteria_amendments.jsonl` (op, from, to, a required one-sentence reason, the versions, the fingerprint, the counts) and a `PROTOCOL_CRITERIA_AMENDED` journal row; `protocol.json`, the corpus snapshot and the batch files are not touched. The criteria in force are the signed list plus the amendments under the current fingerprint. Each amendment raises a criteria version, which the engine stamps on every new decision event, pilot decision and draft. *Changes:* extends H-screening-drafts item 2 (values in force = signed + amendments) from the method to the criteria. *Not chosen:* a new signed protocol version; both.
2. **Decisions already made are kept; a revision shows again** every Include, every Unclear and every Exclude citing a criterion it touched (accepted, as recommended). Since item 5 this applies to counted decisions (the main screening's) only; a revision before the main screening starts shows nothing again. A batch holding a record shown again cannot be approved.
3. **Criterion ids:** a reword keeps the id; split, merge and remove retire it; a new id takes the next number on its side, and **an id is never reused**; a new decision citing a retired id is refused (`REASON_CODE_RETIRED`) (accepted, as recommended).
4. **A revision is allowed from batch preparation until the first batch is approved**, then refused (`CRITERIA_AMENDMENT_AFTER_APPROVAL`); before batches it is refused too (`CRITERIA_AMENDMENT_NO_BATCHES`) (accepted, as recommended). The first draft's "before batches, the criteria change in Inception" is withdrawn: once a corpus is accepted, a change there unbinds the corpus.
5. **The pilot's decisions are not counted** (answered **against the recommendation**: "Pilot records are screened again in the main screening. Cleaner separation, double work on those records."). Unchanged: 50 records by default (20 to 100), simple random with a recorded seed, `literature/screening/pilot/pilot_NNN.json`, `SCREENING_PILOT_DRAWN`, a second pilot allowed. *Proposed rework:* pilot decisions are not decision events; they go to `pilot_NNN_decisions.jsonl` (ids `PLD-…`) through `POST /screening/pilot/{id}/decisions`, which reuses the event intake's checks; no counted reader (projection, queue counts, approval, collect, PRISMA, agreement, the M9b gate) reads them. Not events with a flag, because the screening API contract makes a differing second event a `conflict` that blocks collect, and a reader that forgot the flag would count them. While a pilot is open its records cannot be decided in the main screening (`PILOT_OPEN`); after it they return as ordinary records, blind to the pilot decision. No conflict with Contract v1 provenance was found (pilot decisions never reach the collector; the main decision has no parent and is not dual screening); one with the harness screening API contract is item 11.
6. **During the pilot the Triage checklist stays closed as today** (answered **against the recommendation**: "The result then leans on the agent's answers only."). Consistent with 2026-11-09, "the checklist is a tool for the reason". *Proposed rework:* from the decisions alone the result shows many Unclear, does most of the work and never used (weaker form); hard to judge, fire together, mirrored pair and read differently need the agent check (or checklist answers when given), and the screen names them when they are not computed. "Read differently" without answers compares the agent with what a decision implies, labelled as such.
7. **The lint is plain rules, no model** (accepted, as recommended): five wording checks at Inception, never blockers; seven readings after the pilot.
8. **No not-reported rule is pre-selected at step 5; the researcher must choose** (answered **against the recommendation**). Draft visibility stays pre-selected "after you decide" (proposed; not asked separately). *Proposed rework:* a blocker `METHOD_RULE_UNCHOSEN` at step 5, Review and genesis, also for drafts started before part D and imports without a method block; the step API accepts only the three values; the MCP assistant gets no proposal kind for it; the choice is stored in `intent.json`, so that a later search amendment (M10c 9(a), M11a) is not refused `outside_search`; existing protocols are neither changed nor asked. *Changes:* H-screening-drafts item 1 ("`unclear` stays the default and the value genesis writes") and the 2026-11-08 sentence "New workspaces are written with the block, filled with today's values", for the not-reported value of reviews made at step 5; closes the follow-up of H-screening-drafts item 9. The model default stays `unclear`.
9. **Check the agent on the pilot** (accepted, as recommended) through `POST /proposals/calibrations` with an explicit model, always. *Proposed rework for item 5:* the route gains an additive `pilot_id`, because it refuses records without a counted decision. Methods: "piloted on n records drawn at random (seed …); the pilot decisions were not counted; these records were screened again with the rest of the corpus", then each amendment.
10. **Parts and order A, B, C, then D** (accepted, as recommended; D after M11a part C is merged). Re-sized: A large (unchanged), B large (was medium: a second decision store and intake), C medium, D medium (was small to medium). The build starts when the researcher says.
11. **Answered "Counts only" (as recommended, 2026-11-21; from items 5 and 9): the pilot result never shows the agent's draft for one record.** It meets the screening API contract (accepted 2026-11-04, §3.1): "`proposal_shown` is `null` when the researcher decided before any proposal was revealed. With blinding on (the default) it is always `null` at decision time" and "A reveal (M1b-0) is the first time `GET /records/{id}/proposal` returns a proposal to a screener." Chosen: counts only, never per record, and the main screening shows nothing of the pilot decision, so the contract does not change. *Not chosen:* a draft on request recorded as a reveal; show freely and report those records as not blind (each amends the contract).
12. **Answered "Yes, it asks too" (as recommended, 2026-11-21; from item 8): the terminal wizard** (`scholar-harness inception`) asks the not-reported rule with no default, and a scripted run needs `--not-reported <rule>`; a small CLI change in part D.

## 2026-11-21 — M12a K0: `download` and `ingest` production, the four repairs as built (items 1 to 9 accepted by the researcher 2026-11-21)

Record: `docs/roadmap/milestones/M12a-charting-table.md`, "Build record", Part K0. Builds items 21 and 27 of the M12a entry below ("Production from now"; "Label plus four repairs"). These are the build's own choices where the specification left the detail open; none is accepted until the researcher answers. Nothing here changes a Contract v1 file or `protocol.json`.

1. **`SCHOLAR_MAILTO` wins over the kit's `MAILTO`** when both are set (`scholar_pdf.config.contact_email`): the app's setting is the one the researcher keeps; `MAILTO` stays for kit-only users. *Alternative:* `MAILTO` first (the kit's own name).
2. **`ingest` needs the e-mail too.** It looks the DOI up in OpenAlex and Unpaywall for metadata, so it sends the address; without one it is refused like `download`. *Alternative:* let `ingest` run without the lookups.
3. **The publisher patterns: further than "off by default".** The setting defaults to false *and* the downloader no longer reads it (only the providers' own open-access location is requested, whatever the setting says); `download` and `ingest` refuse the setting when it is true (`PUBLISHER_PATTERNS_REFUSED`, naming item 24), as item 25 does for the proxy, rather than ignoring it silently. This makes DoD K3 ("even when the settings ask for them") true from K0. The module, its exports and its own tests stay until Part K deletes them (item 24); the downloader test that asserted the guessed IEEE URL is replaced by one that asserts no such request. *Alternative:* default false only, the setting still honoured when true.
4. **The refusals are messages of the command, not console refusals:** `MAILTO_NOT_SET` (the code discovery uses), `PROXY_REFUSED`, `PUBLISHER_PATTERNS_REFUSED`; printed with the decision item, exit code 1, checked before any request in the order proxy, patterns, e-mail. No console route reaches the kit in K0, so `console/refusals.py` is unchanged.
5. **The proxy refusal is at the commands; the library no longer reads `PROXY_URL`.** `download` and `ingest` refuse a set proxy. The library's `AsyncPDFDownloader` keeps the parked gateway code, used only when a caller passes a gateway explicitly (its test is kept, without the guessed-URL step); its old fallback to the `PROXY_URL` setting is removed (review of K0, finding 2), so a setting in the environment or a `.env` reaches no caller. Part K decides the rest (item 25: "the code stays parked"). The library refuses a missing e-mail (`MailtoNotSetError` from `process_doi` and `ingest_pdf` before any request).
6. **The user agent is `nexus-scholar/<version> (mailto:<address>)` on every kit request**, the PDF download and the OpenAlex and Unpaywall lookups alike (the lookups pass it per request, since the shared HTTP client only knows `SCHOLAR_MAILTO`). The browser-like `Accept` and `Accept-Language` headers go with the browser user agent; `Accept` is `application/pdf,*/*;q=0.8`.
7. **The help words.** Group: "Production: 'download' (open-access PDFs by DOI) and 'ingest' (a PDF you already have). Reading PDFs ('extract', 'extract-run') and E1 'acquire' are parked". `extract` and `extract-run` begin "PARKED, not production (reading PDFs waits for M12b-read)."; `acquire` begins "PARKED, not production (E1 acquisition stays parked, M12a item 23)." The parked-label test checks each command's own help.
8. **The kit's tests run with a test contact** (`tests/kits/pdf/conftest.py`, autouse), so a developer's own `SCHOLAR_MAILTO` or `MAILTO` never decides a result; the refusal tests blank both.
9. **The parked console pipeline buttons** (G5, item 26), which run `scholar-pdf download`, now refuse without a contact e-mail like any other caller. They stay parked; no change to them.

**Lane RELEASE** (a public command's network behaviour changes).

## 2026-11-21 — M12c agent-proposed cells: the specification's choices (items 1 to 9 answered by the researcher 2026-11-21: 1, 2, 4, 5, 8 and 9 accepted as recommended; 3, 6 and 7 changed; items 10 and 11 accepted 2026-11-21 on N1 and N2, as recommended; a plan only, nothing approved to build or spend)

Asked for by the researcher on 2026-11-21 (M12a question 9, "Plan it now"). Record: `docs/roadmap/milestones/M12c-agent-cells.md`; the researcher's page: `M12c-agent-cells-for-review.md`. Any AI spend needs the researcher's own approval.

The researcher's answers, quoted as chosen: 1 "Yes, contract first"; 2 "Yes"; 3 "Both from the start"; 4 "All, after consent"; 5 "Beside the cell, never in it"; 6 "All columns at once"; 7 "no time to chart by hand just do it without measuring", then, asked whether the app should count as they work, "Count as I work"; 8 "Yes, I type it"; 9 "After M12a, before M9b–M9d". Question n answers item n.

1. *(Accepted.)* **Contract first:** proposals and cells become typed Contract v1 records in a part of their own (M12c-0) before any proposal is adopted.
2. *(Accepted; its shape without a mode or a calibration run is item 11.)* **Provenance:** an adopted value is an `AGENT_ADOPTED` cell event with `screener_id` `agent:<model>` and its `proposal_id`, minted by the engine when the researcher presses Adopt; an edited value is `HUMAN` and names the proposal it replaced.
3. *(Changed by answer 3, not as recommended.)* **Both paths from the first release:** app runs reusing `agent_runs` (estimate, typed ceiling, hard ceiling, tokens and cost recorded) and two MCP tools on the same proposal intake and checks (a study's columns and full text handed out only after consent; the model recorded as the client declares it; the app sees no cost on this path). Part M12c-3, RELEASE, size M. ~~Runs from the app first; MCP later.~~
4. *(Accepted.)* **Sending full texts** needs a consent of its own per provider, and one for "your AI client" on the MCP path, naming what is sent and listing each study's licence; an "openly licensed only" choice on the run screen.
5. *(Accepted; changed by answer 7 only in that nothing is hidden.)* **A proposal is shown beside its cell, never in it**, with its quote highlighted on its page. ~~Hidden during the measurement.~~ The plan's screening blinding default (§2) is read as screening's, not charting's.
6. *(Changed by answer 6, not as recommended.)* **All columns at once:** one switch for the whole form, off by default, journaled. ~~Columns opened one by one, each after its measurement.~~ No accepted decision is affected: every cell is still adopted by the researcher one at a time (the ladder's L0).
7. *(Changed by answer 7, not as recommended.)* **No measurement; counts as the researcher works:** per column, proposals made, failed checks, adopted unchanged, edited, rejected, the agent's "not reported" typed as Not reported or overridden with a value, cannot decide, and cells filled with no proposal. The counts show how much the researcher changed, not the agent's accuracy; a value the agent missed is caught only where the researcher looks for it while typing; the methods text says the accuracy was not measured against independent charting and that each proposal was seen before the reviewer's value. Part M12c-1 is dropped. ~~At least 20 charted studies, blind, thresholds per column.~~
8. *(Accepted.)* **An agent's "not reported" is never adoptable with one key**; checks per proposal: schema, one answer per column, fits the column, `quote_in_source` over the full text with its page (over MCP the engine finds the offsets of the quote the client sends), text quality; a failed check cannot be adopted with one key; no bulk adoption.
9. *(Accepted.)* **Order:** M12b-read (RELEASE, L), M12c-0 contract (RELEASE, M), M12c-2 propose and adopt from the app (RELEASE, L), M12c-3 the MCP path (RELEASE, M); after M12a and before M9b to M9d; the agent is offered once M12c-3 is in.
10. *(Accepted 2026-11-21, N1 "Small eval set"; the run's spend needs the researcher's separate approval.)* **An eval set for the charting task** of about five open-access papers outside the review, values written by the developer for a two-column test form, run once per task version (a small STANDARD part and a spend the researcher approves), because the 2026-11-03 decision "Agent system: proposals only, least autonomy per job" gives every task "an eval set" and "an eval re-run" on change; it checks that the task works, not that it is right on the review. Alternative: no eval set, with a note on that decision.
11. *(Accepted 2026-11-21, N2 "AGENT_ADOPTED, stated plainly".)* **An adopted cell's `AGENT_ADOPTED` event** carries the proposal, the researcher as the adopter, "adopted one at a time" where the ladder's screening event carries a mode, and "none" where it carries a calibration run; the engine mints it, a client never posts it; the ladder decision of 2026-11-10, item 1, is unchanged for screening. Alternative: record the cell as `HUMAN` naming the proposal.

## 2026-11-21 — M12a charting table: the specification's choices (questions 1 to 10 answered by the researcher 2026-11-21: items 1 to 4, 6, 8, 11 and 13 accepted as recommended; item 7 changed by answer 6; item 12 changed by answer 9; items 5, 9 and 10 accepted on the follow-up question 1; items 14 to 20 answered on the follow-up questions F1 to F7, items 15, 20 and 14 changed by F1, F2 and F7; items 22 to 26 accepted on G1 to G5, all as recommended; item 21 changed, "Production from now"; item 27 accepted, "Label plus four repairs")

The researcher's answers, quoted as chosen: 1 "Outside for now"; 2 "Beside the protocol"; 3 "Yes"; 4 "Only after publication"; 5 "Yes, smallest form"; 6 "Fetch open-access PDFs", placed "Own part after A"; 7 "Yes to both"; 8 "Add openpyxl"; 9 "Plan it now"; 10 "A, B, C; CSV in B". Question n answers item: 1→1, 2→2, 3→3, 4→4, 5→6, 6→7, 7→8, 8→11, 9→12, 10→13.

The follow-up answers, quoted as chosen: 1 "Accept all three" (items 5, 9, 10); F1 "Un-park the whole kit" (item 15; not as recommended; the option read "The kit's downloader and commands become production too. Larger review and more risk; not sized"); F2 "B before F" (item 20; not as recommended); F3 "Best open copy, labelled" (item 19); F4 "One at a time" (item 18); F5 "Leave it out" (item 19; asked after F1, with the note that the kit has proxy code); F6 "Yes" (item 14); F7 "Look up by arXiv id now" (item 14; not as recommended). On what "the whole kit" covers, all as recommended: G1 "Reading waits for M12b-read"; G2 "Keep it parked"; G3 "Remove them"; G4 "Refuse with a message"; G5 "Yes, they stay parked" (items 22 to 26). Part K therefore covers downloading only (the downloader, `download`, `ingest`), RELEASE, size L, one PR.

Record: `docs/roadmap/milestones/M12a-charting-table.md`; the researcher's page: `M12a-charting-table-for-review.md`; screens (drafts): `docs/ux/phase3-extraction/final/01-charting-table.md`, `02-one-study.md`. Code checked at `1f218054`. Nothing here changes a Contract v1 model, schema, identity or golden fixture, or `protocol.json`.

1. *(Accepted 2026-11-21, answer 1.)* **Charting and full-text records stay outside Contract v1 for now.** They are workspace records (`charting/events/<screener_id>.jsonl`, `charting/form.json`, `charting/form_amendments.jsonl`, `charting/prisma_fulltext.json`), each event bound to the protocol fingerprint, the screening generation id and fingerprint, and the form version; the export is labelled a working export. A contract decision (typed full-text decision and cell, lineage) comes before an agent proposes cells or a second reviewer joins. *Why:* the table is needed within two weeks; the same pattern holds for the method amendments (H-screening-drafts) and the query development files (M11a). *Alternative:* typed Contract v1 artifacts now (about one more PR).
2. *(Accepted, answer 2.)* **Column options and the Yes/No type live in a charting form beside the protocol**, seeded once from `matrix_dimensions` (id, name, description, `data_type`, `required`); `protocol.json` and its fingerprint are never changed by charting. *Why:* `MatrixDimension` has no field for options, the columns are in the fingerprint, and the M10c amendment path cannot reach them after the corpus is accepted. *Alternative:* add options to the protocol model (schema change, moved fingerprints).
3. *(Accepted, answer 3; widened by F1, item 15; the reading side is item 22.)* **This un-parks, as production:** full-text retrieval status, the full-text decision with one reason, and the hand-filled charting table with its export (the "full-text" item and a manual slice of the Matrix in plan §8). **Stays parked:** fetching and reading PDFs (`scholar_pdf`, PDF-07, PDF-08), stages 5 to 9 of `nexus-scholar run` and `extract`, the automatic matrix extractor (`scholar_rag`, X3, X4), synthesis, the graph, `scholar_verify` and the trust score. Inception's line "Parked: the Matrix phase that fills these columns is not in production." becomes "These columns are filled on the Charting step." *Reads* the decision "2026-11-20 — The order of work after the acceptance run", item 7, as written.
4. *(Accepted, answer 4.)* **The table lists every record published as Include or as Unclear → full text (`MAYBE` in `included.json`), from every published round, and only after publication.** A record that leaves the kept set when screening is published again keeps its values, is marked "No longer kept", and is left out of counts and exports. *Alternative:* a read-only preview from unpublished decisions.
5. *(Accepted 2026-11-21, follow-up question 1, "Accept all three".)* **Full-text status:** To get, Requested (optional date), Retrieved, Not retrieved with exactly one reason (No access; Not found; Authors did not reply; Other, with words). Set for many records at once; one event per record and one journal row per action.
6. *(Accepted, answer 5.)* **Full-text screening is part of M12a, in its smallest form:** Include, or Exclude with exactly one reason (the protocol's `EXC-n` and `FAILS-INC-n` codes, or Other with words), one study at a time (never in bulk), undoable, with no approval step. Counted as **reports** (no linking of reports to studies yet). *Why:* the protocol already says `two_tier_screening: true`; without it the table holds excluded papers and the PRISMA full-text box stays empty. *Alternative:* a milestone of its own with the triage screen.
7. *(Changed by answer 6, "Fetch open-access PDFs", "Own part after A".)* **Part A fetches nothing:** the researcher attaches a local PDF (copied to `pdfs/<study_id>.pdf`, size and SHA-256 recorded, PDF signature checked, at most 100 MB) or pastes a link, and no Part A route sends an outgoing request. **A Part F of its own, after A, fetches open-access copies**, records each file in a manifest, and shows every fetched PDF "to confirm" before it counts as Retrieved (items 14 to 20).
8. *(Accepted, answer 7.)* **Cells:** five types (Text, Number, One of, Several of, Yes/No); a cell is empty, a value, Not reported or Not applicable; an optional note and "where from" per cell; charting without a retrieved full text is allowed and marked "No full text" in the screen, the export and the methods text.
9. *(Accepted 2026-11-21, follow-up question 1, "Accept all three".)* **Pilot and amendments:** a Pilot switch (on at first visit); after any value exists, a change of the form needs a one-sentence reason and writes `charting/form_amendments.jsonl` and `CHARTING_FORM_AMENDED`; affected cells are marked "Chart again" with their history kept; the methods text lists the amendments.
10. *(Accepted 2026-11-21, follow-up question 1, "Accept all three".)* **Saving and audit:** every change is an append-only event (client `event_id`, screener id, time, `method: "HUMAN"`), undo is a later event that `supersedes` it (the screening rule), one `CHARTING_EVENTS_RECEIVED` journal row per save request, plus journal rows for the form, its amendments, the end of the pilot, each attachment and each export (with the file's SHA-256).
11. *(Accepted, answer 8.)* **Export:** CSV (the charting table, `render_csv`) and an .xlsx (sheets Charting, Full text, PRISMA counts, Charting form, About) written with `openpyxl` added to the base install; PRISMA full-text counts per round and in total; a methods block of computed sentences beside screening's. Screening's own `prisma_report.json` and methods paragraph are not changed. Exports never block.
12. *(Changed by answer 9, "Plan it now".)* **No agent in M12a's build; its specification is written now**, in parallel with the build (`M12c-agent-cells.md`, the entry above), as a plan only. Before one is built: full text as text with page anchors (PDF-07 and PDF-08 fixed), a quote check over the full text like screening's `quote_in_source`, the M8b highlighter on it, a measurement on the researcher's own charted rows with an approved spend, item 1's contract decision, and an adoption that is never recorded as `HUMAN`.
13. *(Accepted, answer 10; with answers 6, F1 and F2 the order is A, B, F, C, with K beside B.)* **Parts, each RELEASE:** A rows and full text, B form and cells, K the PDF kit's acquisition, F open-access fetching, C export and reporting; a plain CSV of the table moves into B.
14. *(Accepted 2026-11-21: F6 "Yes" as recommended; changed by F7, "Look up by arXiv id now".)* **Part F's run:** started by the researcher only (`L` on the selection, with the count, the services and the e-mail named); one run per workspace (`FETCH_ALREADY_ACTIVE`), cancellable; Unpaywall with `SCHOLAR_MAILTO` (refused `MAILTO_NOT_SET` without it; the kit's placeholder e-mail never sent); OpenAlex only when `SCHOLAR_OPENALEX_KEY` is set; **a record with no DOI but an arXiv id is asked of arXiv's API and fetched from the `pdf` link arXiv returns, at one request every three seconds (`ARXIV_RATE`), labelled "preprint (arXiv vN)", and sent to Unpaywall too when arXiv's answer carries a journal DOI; the provider's parsing of old-style ids (`arxiv.py:121`) is fixed first; records with neither id are skipped and counted**; the app's own user agent with the e-mail; one request at a time per host; 403, 404 and 410 never retried; a 429 waits out `Retry-After` within the existing cap or ends "Try later". ~~records without a DOI skipped and counted~~
15. *(Changed by F1, "Un-park the whole kit", not as recommended.)* **What Part F un-parks:** the PDF kit's acquisition side becomes production in a part of its own, **Part K** (record (d3)): the contact e-mail required, the app's own user agent, no guessed publisher URLs, the OpenAlex key, no proxy, definitive answers not retried, open-access status from the evidence, the kit's manifest (PDF-07, the kit's half), the identity check (PDF-22), honest commands (PDF-15, -17, -18, -05), portable writes (PDF-03), notices, docs and tests; RELEASE, size L, one PR, built beside B. Part F calls the repaired downloader as a library and builds no fetch module of its own. Where "the whole kit" meets earlier decisions is items 22 to 26. ~~A new production fetch module reusing three pieces of `scholar_pdf`; the downloader, CLI and MCP tools stay parked.~~
16. *(Follows answer 6 and F1; the kit writes the rows, Part F gives the path.)* **The manifest first:** `charting/fetch_manifest.jsonl`, append-only, one row per record a run reaches (provider answers, location, version, licence, redirects, status, bytes, SHA-256, each check); no file stored without its row; files stored as `pdfs/fetched/<sha256>.pdf`; journal `CHARTING_FETCH_STARTED` and `CHARTING_FETCH_FINISHED` with counts, never the key.
